# Correctness / CI-debt open items -- [T] Tracked tier -- crates.io publish-readiness: metadata, naming, dependencies, NO-GO audits

**Part of the split index.** This file holds the full text of every **[T]**
(tracked, not yet actioned) card whose subject matches this file's own
criterion (below). Start at `docs/CORRECTNESS_OPEN_ITEMS.md` for the
purpose/scope/convention header and the round-start reading order, and for
the complete item-number to file lookup table; come here for these specific
card bodies. See `docs/correctness-open-items/ACTIVE.md` for the **[A]**
tier, `docs/correctness-open-items/RESOLVED.md` for the closure trail, and
the sibling `[T]`-tier files (`TRACKED_hook_safety.md`, `TRACKED_verification_coverage.md`, `TRACKED_platform_contracts.md`, `TRACKED_ci_gate_coverage.md`, `TRACKED_test_flakiness.md`, `TRACKED_correctness_residuals.md`, `TRACKED_process_record.md`, `TRACKED_misc.md`) for the rest of
the tier.

**Criterion for this file:** A card belongs here if it is about a decision or blocker that gates a crate's crates.io publication -- naming/description/license/dependency one-way-door decisions, semver-coupling decisions, or a NO-GO verdict (and its blocking findings) from an independent pre-publication audit.

**Card count:** 29.

**Why split by theme, not by item-number range (task #1222, 2026-08-20):**
task #1221 (same day) split the former single `TRACKED.md` into four
number-range files, balanced by line count. The owner rejected that split
and asked for a thematic split instead -- grouping cards by what they are
actually ABOUT, derived from reading all 70 cards rather than assumed.
Every citation of this index that points at ONE SPECIFIC ITEM carries
that item's number, in the form `` `docs/CORRECTNESS_OPEN_ITEMS.md`
item N `` -- task #1227 repaired the seven in `aligned-vmem` that did
not, and two outside it were still open as of that task (both are
recorded in the thin index's Structure section). Citations that point
at the FILE as a whole, at a named SECTION, or at a CLASS of items
rather than one item carry no item number and never needed one (task
#1227's finding; until #1236 these headers overclaimed it as a
universal, asserting that no citation ever pointed at anything but
an item number). Only the numbered citations depend on where item
numbers live, and `docs/CORRECTNESS_OPEN_ITEMS.md` (the thin index)
carries the complete, mechanically generated item-N -> file lookup
table covering EVERY `[T]`-tier number (including the `59a`/`59b`
sub-items) that keeps them resolving -- that table, not this file's
name, is what makes the thematic split safe: the lookup is two-hop
(index table, then this file), but mechanical and always correct. No citing-file
count is typed in this header on purpose: the "42+" typed here at
the split was already 43 (census against the split commit) -- #1230
removed it from one of these nine headers, #1236 from the other
eight; compare against this command's output, never a hardcoded
count:

```text
git grep -l "docs/CORRECTNESS_OPEN_ITEMS\.md" -- ':!docs/' | wc -l
```

(Split 2026-08-20, task #1222, superseding task #1221's number-range
split the same day.)

    - **[Added 2026-08-20, task #1207] A SECOND, HARDER blocker for the
      same three crates (plus `numa-shim`), found while investigating the
      release guard: none of the four has a `CHANGELOG.md` AT ALL.**
      Measured directly — `test -f crates/<c>/CHANGELOG.md` returns MISSING
      for `racy-ptr-cell`, `size-classes`, `tagged-index-stack` and
      `numa-shim`; only `aligned-vmem` has one. This is not cosmetic:
      `.github/workflows/release.yml:268-272` resolves the CHANGELOG path
      from `cargo metadata` by package name and then fails CLOSED
      (`::error::CHANGELOG not found at $CHANGELOG` / `exit 1`) — a
      fail-closed branch task #1115 added deliberately. So a publish
      attempt for any of the four stops at that step, before `cargo
      publish` runs. The guard is behaving correctly; the files simply do
      not exist. Owner: **task #1220** (write real per-crate changelogs
      reconstructed from `git log -- crates/<c>/`, not stubs).
    - **[Added 2026-08-20, task #1207] Release-guard status, recorded
      because the task that raised it was based on MY OWN too-narrow
      verification and resolved NULL.** The claim under investigation was
      that `## 0.2.0 - Unreleased` would ship in the tarball with no gate
      catching it. The supporting evidence was `grep -rn "Unreleased"
      scripts/*.mjs .github/workflows/ci.yml` → zero hits, which is TRUE
      but was generalised into "no guard anywhere looks at that word".
      The guard exists: `.github/workflows/release.yml:301-309`, and its
      own comment names `## 0.2.0 - Unreleased` (aligned-vmem) as one of
      the two heading shapes it is written to catch, case-insensitively,
      after an anchored per-version section match that requires exactly one
      section. Counterfactual run against the guard body extracted
      verbatim: with today's header it exits 1 ("Stamp the real release
      date"); with a dated header it exits 0; a CHANGELOG carrying a
      legitimate `## Unreleased` section for FUTURE work alongside a dated
      released section still passes, so it is not a false-positive
      generator. **Recorded decision:** the version header is dated
      immediately before `cargo publish`, not now — dating it today would
      fabricate a release date, which is the exact defect task #1099/I2
      (`5dc4385`) already had to remove once. That decision is held by the
      guard, not by intention. **Honest limitation:** the guard lives only
      in `release.yml` under `dry_run != 'true'`, so neither `npm run
      check` nor `ci.yml` ever exercises it — a contributor sees it fire
      for the first time during a real release. That is not the task-#1131
      "guard nothing runs" class (it does run, at exactly the moment the
      decision above refers to), but it is stated here rather than left to
      read as full CI coverage.

27. **[T, filed 2026-08-06, task #653/P19, `docs/reviews/2026-08-06-publish-readiness-sweep-closing-review.md` finding P3-4 item 3] tagged-index-stack's `compile_error!` guard for unsupported `target_has_atomic` widths doesn't suppress the cascading `E0432` unresolved-import error on the same build — a deliberate, unrecorded tradeoff.**

    - **Status:** OPEN — low-priority polish, deliberately deferred, not an
      oversight. Purely cosmetic (the build already fails either way on an
      unsupported target; the only difference is whether the FIRST error a
      user sees is the clear named-reason `compile_error!` or that error
      followed by a cascade of confusing `E0432`s).
    - **Current-number-or-verdict:** commit
      `300b41f97a0e7c85310e5ed53dcbf289414e779f`'s own body: adding the
      `#[cfg(not(target_has_atomic = "64"))] compile_error!` guard (F2) does
      fire first and gives a clear, named-reason error on an unsupported
      target — a real (if small) behavior improvement. But it does not
      suppress the subsequent cascading `E0432` unresolved-import error
      that still follows on the same build, because `compile_error!` does
      not halt the rest of module compilation. Fully suppressing the
      cascade would require `#[cfg(target_has_atomic = "64")]`-gating every
      downstream item in the file — "judged too intrusive for the benefit
      on an already-broken build," per the commit body.
    - **Why filed instead of fixed here:** it is a conscious, defensible,
      already-reasoned-through tradeoff, not a bug — filing it only so the
      decision is recorded somewhere indexed instead of living solely in
      one commit message.
    - **Next trigger:** none required; revisit only if a future contributor
      finds the cascading `E0432` output genuinely confusing enough in
      practice to justify the `cfg`-gating cost across the file. Low
      priority, no forcing deadline.
    - **Evidence:** commit `300b41f97a0e7c85310e5ed53dcbf289414e779f`'s
      full commit body; `crates/tagged-index-stack/src/lib.rs` (the
      `compile_error!` guard);
      `docs/reviews/2026-08-06-publish-readiness-sweep-closing-review.md`
      finding P3-4 item 3.

28. **[T, filed 2026-08-06, task #653/P19, `docs/reviews/2026-08-06-publish-readiness-sweep-closing-review.md` finding P3-4 item 4] Two one-way-door publish decisions for `racy-ptr-cell` — its name and its 383-character `description` — were surfaced in commit `9ecada3`'s body but never recorded anywhere indexed, and become permanent the moment the crate first publishes.**

      here": (a) the crate name `racy-ptr-cell` reads to a newcomer as "has
      data races" — the OPPOSITE of the guarantee the crate actually
      provides (a lock-free, race-safe exactly-once cell) — and was
      confirmed free on crates.io as of the original review date (subject

      names can be claimed by others in the interim); (b) `Cargo.toml`'s
      `description` field is 383 characters, long for a crates.io listing
      (crates.io does not hard-limit description length, but long
      descriptions truncate awkwardly in search-result UI). Neither is
      recorded anywhere indexed prior to this filing, nor in K3/#598's own
      task description.
    - **Why filed instead of fixed here:** both are one-way-door naming/
      metadata decisions requiring maintainer judgment (a rename affects
      every existing reference across the workspace and any external
      consumer once published; a description rewrite is a content call) —
      not something to resolve unilaterally in a bookkeeping-only task.
    - **Next trigger:** resolve as part of the deferred publish-DAG pass
      (K3/#598), before `racy-ptr-cell`'s first `cargo publish` — decide
      whether to rename the crate (and if so, to what) and whether to
      shorten `description`, then re-verify crates.io name availability
      immediately before the actual publish action (names can be claimed
      by others between now and then).
    - **Evidence:** commit `9ecada3d25bcbdf33e9b184c4233685e5b6a243f`'s full
      commit body, §"Not addressed here"; `crates/racy-ptr-cell/Cargo.toml`
      (`name`, `description` fields);
      `docs/reviews/2026-08-06-publish-readiness-sweep-closing-review.md`
      finding P3-4 item 4.

29. **[T, filed 2026-08-06, task #654/P20, `docs/reviews/2026-08-06-publish-readiness-sweep-closing-review.md` finding P4-12] `#![deny(missing_docs)]` on 4 about-to-be-published crates is a one-way-door tradeoff versus the more common `warn` + CI `-D warnings` convention — no conscious publish-time decision recorded.**

    - **Status:** OPEN — low severity, pre-publish-decision item. No code
      defect: all four crates compile clean today at 100% doc coverage.
    - **Current-number-or-verdict:** `#![deny(missing_docs)]` was added to
      `racy-ptr-cell` (commit `9ecada3`, task #642) and to `sefer-region`,
      `size-classes`, `tagged-index-stack` (commit `7c8621f`, task #651).
      All four verified at 100% doc coverage as of the commits above, and
      all four compile clean today. The tradeoff: `deny` (vs. the more
      common ecosystem convention of a lib-level `warn` plus CI-level `-D
      warnings`) means that once a crate is PUBLISHED, a future rustc
      release that widens what counts as `missing_docs` would turn a
      downstream consumer's `cargo build` of that already-published,
      pinned version red — with no recourse for the consumer, since they
      cannot edit a crate they don't own. `warn` would not have this
      failure mode (a widened lint would show as a new warning on
      recompilation of the crate's own source, not retroactively break an
      already-published, unmodified version's build for downstream
      consumers). None of these four crates has been published yet, so
      the tradeoff is still avoidable.
    - **Why filed instead of fixed here:** this is a deliberate policy
      choice between two defensible conventions (`deny` vs. `warn` + CI
      gate), not a bug — it needs a conscious maintainer decision before
      first/next publish, not a unilateral edit in a bookkeeping-only task.
    - **Next trigger:** before any of `racy-ptr-cell` / `sefer-region` /
      `size-classes` / `tagged-index-stack`'s first (or next) `cargo
      publish`, decide whether to keep `#![deny(missing_docs)]` as-is
      (accepting the one-way-door risk) or downgrade to `#![warn(missing_docs)]`
      plus an equivalent CI-level `-D warnings` gate (matching the more
      common ecosystem convention, avoiding the retroactive-break failure
      mode). Natural to fold into the deferred publish-DAG pass (K3/#598).
    - **Evidence:** commit `9ecada3d25bcbdf33e9b184c4233685e5b6a243f`
      (`racy-ptr-cell`); commit `7c8621f` (`sefer-region`, `size-classes`,
      `tagged-index-stack`); `crates/racy-ptr-cell/src/lib.rs`,
      `crates/sefer-region/src/lib.rs` (the `sefer-region` package),
      `crates/size-classes/src/lib.rs`,
      `crates/tagged-index-stack/src/lib.rs` (each crate's
      `#![deny(missing_docs)]` attribute);
      `docs/reviews/2026-08-06-publish-readiness-sweep-closing-review.md`
      finding P4-12.

85. **[T, filed 2026-08-11, task #821, renumbered from 46 by task #1143 (2026-08-19) — a duplicate item number, not a new item] `crates/sefer-region` depends on `captrack`
    v0.1.1 (exact-pinned) as a dev-dependency for a single ignored probe test.**
    **Renumbering note (task #1143):** this card was originally filed as item 46 by commit `7ee57a9` (2026-08-11), which appended it at the file's then-current end without checking that 46 was already taken — the numa-shim `reserve_on_node` item (filed 2026-08-09, task #778, two days earlier) had legitimately owned number 46 since before this card existed (confirmed via `git show 7ee57a9~1:docs/CORRECTNESS_OPEN_ITEMS.md`, which shows item 46 = numa-shim at that parent commit). The duplicate persisted through every later edit, including the R34-24 archive split, because the pairing test (`tests/no_stale_doc_references.rs::correctness_index_recently_resolved_pointers_carry_verdicts`) only checks the "Recently resolved" section's pointers, not active-tier item numbers, so nothing caught it. Renumbered to 85 (the next number above the highest then in use, 84) rather than shifting every intervening item, to avoid the exact cascading-renumber risk task #1109's index split already demonstrated (9 pointers truncated, 19 of 32 verdicts lost) when a purely mechanical edit touched more of the file than intended. No other document in this repo cites this card as "item 46" — every other reference to "item 46" in `docs/`, including all checkpoints, means the DIFFERENT numa-shim item in this same file, or `docs/perf/OPEN_ITEMS.md`'s own unrelated former item 46 (the Unix exact-reserve hit-rate item, since closed and moved to that file's "Recently resolved"); this card's own content is referenced elsewhere only by task number (#821) or commit (`7ee57a9`), never by item number, so the renumbering breaks no cross-reference (verified by `git grep -n "item 46"` across `docs/` — see below).
    `captrack` is a heavy dependency (proc macro, `ctor` constructor with a
    background thread, supply-chain side effects). The workspace-root
    `.cargo/config.toml` suppresses autodump via `CAPTRACK_AUTODUMP=0`, but that
    config does NOT travel with the published crate tarball — external consumers
    who build `sefer-region`'s tests will see the ctor run at process startup
    without that suppression. The dependency is intentional (the probe provides
    capacity telemetry for `Region<T>`'s `slotmap::SlotMap` backing that cannot
    be obtained any other way — see `tests/captrack_probe.rs`'s module doc), and
    a std-only alternative was drafted and explicitly declined in this task
    (the user decided to keep captrack rather than rewrite the probe). The
    mitigation applied is exact version pinning (`=0.1.1`), which eliminates the
    risk that a future minor/patch bump of captrack silently changes its
    side-effect profile without an explicit review.

    **Standalone-build verification (empirical, not reasoned):** A fresh copy
    of `crates/sefer-region/` was extracted to `C:\temp\sefer-region-standalone-test`,
    outside this workspace (no parent `[workspace]` Cargo.toml, no
    `.cargo/config.toml` with `CAPTRACK_AUTODUMP=0`). Commands run:
    - `cargo build --tests` — clean compile, no visible side effect
    - `cargo test --no-default-features --features std` — all tests passed,
      `captrack_probe` correctly ignored
    - No stray JSON files, no persistent background processes observed (the
      ctor's behavior was not directly instrumented, only its visible side
      effects; absence of obvious artifacts is NOT proof of absence, but it is
      the empirical baseline recorded here)

    **Status:** OPEN — the dependency and probe remain in place per explicit
    user decision; only exact-pinning mitigation was applied (not removal,
    not std-only replacement).

    **Next trigger:** revisit if captrack's own side-effect profile changes
    (e.g., future releases introduce additional ctor activity), or if a future
    round decides to swap it for the std-only alternative that was drafted and
    declined in task #821.

90. **[T, second instance of item 89's class — a NO-GO review landed with zero index cards] Commit `967b821` committed `docs/reviews/2026-08-19-aligned-vmem-publication-readiness-audit-r2.md` (independent read-only re-audit of `aligned-vmem`, verdict **NO-GO for publishing 0.2.0** — 0 Critical, 0 High, 4 Medium, 3 Low, 3 perf candidates, 1 coverage gap) and, per its own body, "filed as #1171 (decision), with #1172/#1173/#1174 sequenced behind it" — but filed no card in this index, `docs/perf/OPEN_ITEMS.md`, or either archive. Filed here (task #1175) exactly one round after item 89 named the same "filed as a follow-up" pattern in a different script, confirming it is a recurring class, not a one-off.** (Filed 2026-08-19, task #1175.)

    - **Status (refreshed 2026-08-23, task #1251 — the seventh independent audit's G2 finding):** All four owners are LANDED, AND the single OPEN QUESTION below is now ANSWERED — see the appended answer directly under it (task #1190, decided 2026-08-20: **NO**). Nothing this card itself still tracks is pending. **The line this replaces** (refreshed 2026-08-20, task #1208) said *"This card closes when #1190 is answered"* — true when written, but #1190's answer landed inside this SAME card, three lines below, that same day, and the Status line above it was never refreshed to say so; the seventh independent audit caught the three-day-old gap (`docs/reviews/2026-08-23-133616-aligned-vmem-readonly-review-GLM.md`, finding G2). **Not moved to "Recently resolved"**: the crate's 0.2.0 NO-GO still stands, but solely on item 97/98's F1 (the deliberately-deferred `CHANGELOG.md` `## 0.2.0 - Unreleased` header) — nothing item 90 itself owns remains open.
      **The line this replaces said "None of #1172/#1180/#1173/#1174 has landed yet", and stayed true-then/false-later for a full wave** — it was accurate when written (2026-08-19, task #1171) and was invalidated by the four landings without anyone updating it. That is the exact staleness class R34-24 created current-state cards to prevent, and the reason task #1208 exists as a separate, deliberately-LAST task in its wave: refreshing a Status line mid-wave only re-stales it (the task-#1193 lesson).
    - **Current-verdict:** `git grep -n "2026-08-19-aligned-vmem-publication-readiness-audit-r2"` returned zero hits before this edit (re-verified 2026-08-19, task #1175) — the review file existed, was fully committed (203 lines), and was referenced nowhere. `grep -n "from_raw_parts" docs/CORRECTNESS_OPEN_ITEMS.md` before this edit matched only item 53 (CLOSED, a different `granted_huge` finding from the 2026-08-14 pre-release review, not this one). Report is read-only by its own stated scope ("Режим: только чтение... без запуска тестов, сборки, cargo check, Clippy, Miri, benchmark и `cargo publish --dry-run`") — its findings below are UNVERIFIED BY EXECUTION, only by source reading; treat accordingly.
    - **The four Medium findings (report §M1–M4, `docs/reviews/2026-08-19-aligned-vmem-publication-readiness-audit-r2.md:39-117`), each blocking 0.2.0 publication because a post-publication fix would need a semver-incompatible change:**
      - **M1** — `Reservation::from_raw_parts`'s `granted_huge: bool` cannot describe an arbitrary adopted HugeTLB mapping (no encoded huge-page size or `munmap` granularity requirement); `Drop` silently leaks the whole mapping if `reservation_len` is not huge-page-multiple.
      - **M2** — an already-adopted huge mapping's decommit behavior depends on the CONSUMER's Cargo feature set: `is_huge()` is available without `huge-pages`, but the eligible-forward `MADV_DONTNEED` path is feature-gated, so the same live mapping is served or silently skipped depending on the caller's crate composition.
      - **M3** — `from_raw_parts`'s `# Safety` section mixes true memory-safety preconditions with functional/behavioral ones; the crate's own integration tests deliberately violate some of the mixed-in conditions while explaining why doing so is not UB — a self-contradictory contract.
      - **M4** — `try_decommit() -> Result<(), VmemError>` reports range validity, not decommit outcome; `Ok(())` cannot distinguish a Rust-level skip, kernel-rejected `madvise`/`VirtualFree`, accepted advisory, or actual RSS reclaim, despite rustdoc for `can_decommit_reclaim_and_zero()` suggesting the return value can be used to judge exactly that.
    - **Three Low findings (report §L1–L3, lines 119-146):** **L1** — poison-state `decommit()` is documented as a no-op but `debug_assert!(false, ...)` makes it a debug-build panic, undocumented in `# Panics` and unpinned by any test oracle. **L2** — `VmemError`'s single no-code sentinel is overloaded across at least six distinct causes (invalid args / OS refusal-no-code / zero grant / page-size query failure / fault-injection / mock backend), and `os_refusal_unknown_code`'s "FOUR sources" doc claim does not enumerate the real call sites. **L3** — **already covered by item 88 above (MSRV does not compile `aligned-vmem/huge-pages` — no `-p aligned-vmem` row in the `msrv` CI job); not a duplicate card, only cross-referenced here as the same gap re-surfacing from a second source.**
    - **Coverage gap (report, "Coverage gap" section, lines 170-174):** the real-HugeTLB CI job proves `MAP_HUGETLB` grant + eligible `madvise` dispatch, but reads no memory post-decommit and measures no RSS/pool reclaim — it distinguishes "kernel accepted the advice" from nothing, not from "memory was actually returned."
    - **This header's "3 perf candidates" (report §P1-P3), owners and content:** filed in `docs/perf/OPEN_ITEMS.md` item 57 (task #1182) — the perf-scoped index this material's own Scope belongs to, which the commit landing this card did not touch.

Recorded here because the non-retroactive convention this file follows is worth more than a tidy claim about having followed it; the in-place edit itself is left standing, since reverting it would restore a figure task #1196 correctly showed to be misleading.
      - **M1 — HYBRID** (none of the report's own options): keep `granted_huge: bool`, but narrow what `true` MEANS to "the mapping is in the crate's own 2 MiB format": (a) `from_raw_parts` asserts, on Linux/Android, that `len`, `reservation_len`, `reservation`, `base`, and the offset `base − reservation` are ALL 2 MiB multiples when `granted_huge == true`; (b) accepting `granted_huge: true` is gated behind the `huge-pages` feature — this CLOSES M2 as a consequence (with no feature there are no huge-flagged adopted mappings at all, so there is no divergent behavior to have); (c) rewrite the `reservation_len` rounding bullet (`crates/aligned-vmem/src/reservation.rs:1071-1078`) to state the HugeTLB exception explicitly. REJECTED, conditionally on the open question below: typed huge-granularity metadata — over-engineering while the crate implements exactly one size, `LINUX_HUGE_PAGE_SIZE = 2 MiB`.
        **Appended note (task #1196/OX6-L1, decision text above left verbatim per this file's non-retroactive convention):** the five names in (a) are not five independent checks — `reservation` and `base` both being 2-MiB multiples already implies their difference `base − reservation` is too, so the offset conjunct can never be the one that fails; the assert as SHIPPED (`crates/aligned-vmem/src/reservation.rs`) has FOUR independent checks, not five, and the code and its comments now say so. The decision itself is unaffected — the shipped assert still checks exactly the five names listed here; only the "five independent requirements" framing was corrected, not the check.
      - **M3 — SPLIT.** `# Safety` keeps ONLY memory-safety preconditions; the functional requirements (accuracy of `granted_huge`, Windows commit-state) move to a separate "Correctness contract" section stating the precise consequence of violating each. REJECTED: declaring the tested violations UB and rewriting the tests — that would assert something demonstrably false; `tests/granted_huge_reader_enumeration.rs` pins exactly the non-UB behavior.
      - **M4 — `Result<DecommitOutcome, VmemError>`** with `Skipped` / `Advised` / `Refused(VmemError)` behind ONE private helper. `Advised` means "the kernel accepted the advice", explicitly NOT "RSS was reclaimed" — that gap is owned by the coverage-gap item (#1174), not by M4. REJECTED: option (b) — renaming/documenting the function as a range-contract check only.

They generate no remediation work):** (1) "`Drop` silently loses the `munmap` error" is FALSE — under `bench-internals` it IS counted, in `UNIX_MUNMAP_FAILURES` (`crates/aligned-vmem/src/os/unix.rs:1103-1109`, task P2-6). Caveat both sources add: in the ordinary public API the result is still unobservable — a separate question, not an accounting defect. (2) The crate-level "every entry point has two forms" text is correctly scoped by `crates/aligned-vmem/src/lib.rs:55` ("every **reservation/commit** entry point"); the REAL inaccuracy is a DIFFERENT sentence, at `lib.rs:62-63` ("The infallible forms forward to the `try_*` forms"), which is wrong for the decommit family — that fix belongs to task #1172, point (4).

      - **OPEN QUESTION (owner: task #1190 — re-pointed by #1190 itself from #1171, which is DONE per the "Owning follow-up tasks" line above and so must not stand as a live owner per R34-24; raised by the third audit after the decision was taken; ANSWERED 2026-08-20, appended directly below):** does the crate intend to support ADOPTING HugeTLB mappings whose granularity is not 2 MiB (e.g. 1 GiB)? Today's default is NO — the crate nowhere promises such support — and under that default the hybrid stands as decided above. If the answer ever becomes YES, typed huge-granularity metadata must be introduced BEFORE the first publication (afterward it is a semver break), and task #1172's scope grows accordingly.
        **Appended answer (task #1190, owner decision 2026-08-20): NO.** `aligned-vmem` does not support adopting a HugeTLB mapping whose granularity is not 2 MiB; the 2-MiB assert is the contract, not a temporary narrowing. Decided pre-publication, so the `granted_huge: bool` contract is final as shipped — a future "yes", if it ever comes, would be ADDITIVE (a new constructor carrying typed huge-granularity metadata, not a relaxation of the existing assert presented as a bugfix), because `granted_huge: true` already means "this crate's own 2 MiB format" (#1172's narrowing) and stays truthful for that case. Its own text states no new UB was found in the unsafe/FFI/ownership paths reviewed (Linux raw FFI bounds/`mbind` arguments/`maxnode`/close paths; Windows FFI layout/reserve-commit-release ownership/`from_raw_parts`; Linux cpumap parsing bounds; topology cache allocation-free init and `OnceLock` lifetime; Cargo metadata/MSRV/license/docs.rs wiring; CI/release doc platform coverage) — a static confirmation, not an executed one.
    - **[Added 2026-08-23, task #1267 — F5 scope-expanded: the semver policy for BOTH doc-hidden test-only modules (`pub mod cpumap` AND `pub mod linux`), RECOMMENDATION RECORDED, owner decision pending. Docs-only task — no code changed.]** A later zero-trust review of the original F5 finding expanded its scope: the crate now has TWO `#[doc(hidden)] pub mod` declarations, not one — `pub mod cpumap` (`crates/numa-shim/src/lib.rs:550-551` as of this task; `:449` at the audited revision) exporting `format_sysfs_path`/`parse_contains_cpu`/`trim_end`/`nth_token`/`parse_hex_u32`, and `pub mod linux { pub fn dbg_node_resolution_for_cpu }` (`src/lib.rs:691-693`), added by task #1266 AFTER this audit reported, in exactly the same shape (cfg-gated `#[cfg(all(target_os = "linux", not(miri), not(feature = "mock")))]`). `#[doc(hidden)]` hides items from rendered rustdoc; it does not make them private — every function in both modules is callable by any downstream crate today, so whatever the next publish ships becomes semver surface. Both modules exist to give `tests/` integration tests a target-independent oracle (integration-test binaries link the crate as an external crate and see only `pub` items — the compiler-enforced reason this forwarder pattern exists at all), the pattern CLAUDE.md sanctions as exception category 1 under "One file — one export". The audit's narrower point stands: that sanction was written with this repo's never-published root crate in mind, where "not stable public API" is a workable in-repo convention; for a crate already on crates.io it is invisible to an external consumer and unenforced by the compiler. One policy must cover BOTH modules — they are the same shape of problem, and recommending different dispositions for them would be arbitrary.
      **PREMISE CORRECTION (observed, task #1267) — neither module is in published 0.1.0, so the breaking-change door is still OPEN until the next publish.** The audit's own F5 text ("для уже опубликованного `0.1.0` удаление доступного модуля было бы breaking change") and the task-#1267 brief's "removing either module later would be a breaking change to already-published API" both overstate the current constraint. Measured directly: `git show 845560f:crates/numa/src/lib.rs | grep -c "pub mod cpumap"` returns 0, and that pre-publish tree contains zero `doc(hidden)` occurrences at all — `845560f` (2026-06-28) is the tree's last commit before the 2026-06-29 publish, per this crate's own CHANGELOG. `pub mod cpumap` was introduced 2026-08-09 (`c5e013b`, task #721), six weeks AFTER the publish; `pub mod linux` by `3a83ee8` (task #1266). Consequence: options (a) and (b) below are still NON-BREAKING today; the audit's real deadline — decide before the next major/minor publish — is unchanged and now concrete: the already-queued next release (carrying #1266's `NodeResolution` additive API and the `maxnode` fix) is the publish that would freeze these modules as public API, so the decision must land in that release's scope, not after it.
      **The three options, with the tradeoffs that actually bind (same direction required for both modules):**
      - **(a) Move to a separate test-support crate.** Cleanest in principle, but structurally ASYMMETRIC: `cpumap`'s five functions are pure and self-contained, yet the crate's own production topology cache CALLS them at runtime (`crate::cpumap::format_sysfs_path` at `src/lib.rs:867`, `crate::cpumap::parse_contains_cpu` at `:888`), so the parser cannot live in a dev-only crate — (a) for `cpumap` really means extracting it into a new PUBLISHED utility crate that numa-shim depends on normally (new crate, new release pipeline, new docs surface — disproportionate for five small functions). For `linux::dbg_node_resolution_for_cpu` it is not available at all without further restructuring: the function forwards to `pub(crate)` platform internals (`platform::cpu_to_numa_node_checked`, the crate's own topology cache, `src/lib.rs:710-719`) that no external crate can reach. A UNIFORM (a) therefore implies restructuring the platform internals too — a far larger change than the problem warrants.
      - **(b) Keep an internal non-pub test seam plus integration-test restructuring.** Compiler-blocked in its naive form: integration tests in `tests/` see only `pub` items — there is no `pub(crate)`-visibility path from `tests/`, which is exactly why the forwarder pattern exists. Realizing (b) means moving the oracles inline into `src/` behind `#[cfg(test)]` unit tests, which contradicts this repo's standing "tests live in `tests/`, not inline" convention — itself an owner-level convention change stacked on top of the decision this addendum exists to request. For completeness: feature-gating the modules is NOT a fourth escape hatch — a published Cargo feature is itself published surface (item 42's corrected card and the `mock` feature's own corrected `Cargo.toml` comment record exactly this lesson).
      - **(c) Deliberately declare both modules semver-stable and commit to maintaining them.** Zero restructuring, zero convention change, and it matches how the modules are ALREADY maintained: task #727 hardened `format_sysfs_path`'s buffer sizing to the full `u32` signature (`src/lib.rs:562-573`) precisely because it is reachable `pub` — the functions are already held to a public-quality bar with real behavioral oracles (`tests/cpumap_parser.rs`, `tests/node_resolution_linux.rs`). Cost: a permanent maintenance obligation (any signature change becomes a major bump), a `dbg_`-prefixed, mock-conditional item frozen into the stable surface, and — unless accompanied by un-hiding — the incoherent contract "guaranteed forever but deliberately undocumented".
      **Recommendation (task #1267; NOT a decision — do not implement without owner sign-off):** option **(c)** for BOTH modules at the next release, refined per module: promote `pub mod cpumap` OUT of `#[doc(hidden)]` as real, documented, semver-stable API (small, pure, well-tested, genuinely reusable; stable-but-hidden is an incoherent published contract), and commit `linux::dbg_node_resolution_for_cpu` as a semver-stable hidden seam — frozen as-is unless the owner prefers a pre-publish rename to drop the test-flavored `dbg_` prefix, which is free until that publish and costs a major bump after. Grounds: (a) cannot be applied uniformly without restructuring platform internals; (b) cannot be applied at all without first changing the repo's test-placement convention; (c) is the only zero-restructuring option and ratifies the existing maintenance posture rather than changing it. The implementation work this implies (un-hide + rustdoc for `cpumap`, the CHANGELOG's own "test infrastructure (`#[doc(hidden)]`), not public API" line for task #721, any rename) is deliberately NOT done by this docs-only task.
      - **Status:** F5 = DECIDED and EXECUTED (task #1267's writeup recorded a recommendation; task #1289, 2026-08-24, executed the owner's actual decision — a different option than the writeup's recommended (c), see the addendum below — applying to BOTH the `cpumap` module and task #1266's `linux` module; docs-only, no code/visibility change).
      - **Next trigger:** the owner's pick of (a)/(b)/(c) — must land in the scope of the NEXT version publish (the queued release carrying #1266's `NodeResolution`), because that publish is what freezes both modules as crates.io semver surface.
      - **Evidence:** `crates/numa-shim/src/lib.rs:533-549` (cpumap module's own comment block naming the sanctioned pattern), `:550-551` (`#[doc(hidden)] pub mod cpumap`), `:562-573` (task #727's public-quality hardening), `:688-693` (the `linux` module's comment + cfg + `#[doc(hidden)] pub mod linux`), `:710-719` (`dbg_node_resolution_for_cpu` forwarding to `pub(crate)` `platform::cpu_to_numa_node_checked`), `:867,888` (production call sites into `cpumap::`); `git show 845560f:crates/numa/src/lib.rs` (zero `doc(hidden)`, zero `pub mod cpumap` — the published-0.1.0 premise check, OBSERVED); `git log -S "pub mod cpumap"` → `c5e013b` (2026-08-09, task #721); `git log -S "dbg_node_resolution_for_cpu"` → `3a83ee8` (task #1266); audit report `docs/reviews/2026-08-23-164206-numa-shim-publication-audit-Sol-codex.md` line 62 (the three options in its own words); CLAUDE.md "One file — one export" exception category 1; item 42 above (feature-gating is not a semver escape).

105. **[T, FOURTEENTH independent audit] `docs/reviews/2026-08-24-113155-numa-shim-publication-audit-Sol-codex.md` (author Sol-codex; reported 2026-08-24 11:31:55 Europe/Berlin; revision `58d94da80bf829f0675290546021f5916402fb8c`; verdict **NO-GO — one new P1 functional blocker, one already-tracked P1 residual re-confirmed, two release-process blockers already in flight, and several P2 hardening items**) is a STATIC read-only audit (no sub-agents, no tests/build/`cargo check`/Clippy/Miri/`cargo publish` run; GitHub Actions checked only via a read-only metadata query, which found no CI run for the exact HEAD reviewed — accurate, since that push had not happened yet when this audit ran). **This is the first review in the entire 14-audit campaign to find a genuinely new, previously-unidentified P1 functional defect in already-reviewed code** — confirmed independently by this filing task (see below), not merely transcribed.** (Filed 2026-08-24, task #1302.)

    - **Status:** OPEN — refreshed 2026-08-24 by task #1306's own filing addendum below. The headline P1 (`bind_range` broken by design) is CLOSED, by removal (see the `[Added 2026-08-24, task #1306]` addendum below) — implemented, zero-trust reviewed, and independently re-executed by the closing task, not merely re-read. Remaining on this item: the P2 Windows `debug_assert_eq!` hardening (CLOSED separately by task #1304, landed before this item was even filed — task #1302's filing missed that #1304 already addressed part of what this review flagged; recorded here for completeness) and the P3 mock-doc wording fix (CLOSED by task #1305, same situation). The re-confirmed-but-already-tracked node-`>=`-64 residual is now moot — task #1306 converted it from a silent no-op into a typed `InvalidNode` error, closing it as a side effect of the `bind_range` removal rather than as a standalone fix.
    - **Current-number-or-verdict:** NO-GO at `58d94da`, the first NO-GO verdict since item 102 (the eleventh review, `1043b0e`) — items 103/104 (twelfth/thirteenth reviews) had both progressed to CONDITIONAL GO. This audit reopens the readiness question on a genuine functional-correctness ground, not a process/doc-bookkeeping one.
      - **P1 (NEW, CONFIRMED) — Linux `bind_range` silently no-ops for the exact heap-allocation example the crate's own README teaches:** `src/lib.rs:512-529` (`bind_range`) calls `platform::bind_range_impl` → `bind_range_impl_linux` (`:1099-1131`), which passes `base` directly to `mbind(2)` via `libc_mbind(...)` (`:1120-1128`) and **discards the syscall's return value entirely** — no assignment, no `?`, no check, by original design (task #722 era; the doc comment at `:461-463` already says "silently ignores OS errors... the allocation is always valid regardless of whether binding succeeded"). **This filing task independently verified, via the authoritative `man7.org/linux/man-pages/man2/mbind.2.html` ERRORS section (fetched live, not assumed), that `mbind(2)` returns `EINVAL` when `addr` is not a multiple of the system page size — the kernel does NOT round the address down; it rejects the call outright.** `README.md:48-53`'s headline "Bind a live allocation to a NUMA node" example passes `vec![0u8; 4096].as_mut_ptr()` — a plain heap allocation from the global allocator, whose alignment is that allocator's normal small-object alignment (typically 8-16 bytes on the glibc/system allocator for a 4 KiB request, well below `mmap_threshold`), essentially never page-aligned in practice. **Consequence: the crate's headline documented example, copy-pasted verbatim, silently fails to bind on Linux in the overwhelming majority of runs** — `EINVAL` is returned by the kernel, discarded by the crate, and the caller observes ordinary success with no NUMA policy actually applied. `tests/smoke.rs:25-51`'s `bind_range_on_owned_memory_does_not_panic` only asserts the call doesn't panic and the buffer stays readable — it does not (and structurally cannot, without a kernel-side oracle) assert that binding actually took effect, so this gap has zero test coverage in either direction.
      - **P1 (RE-CONFIRMED, ALREADY TRACKED, not new) — node IDs `>= 64` are silently dropped:** `bind_range_impl_linux:1104-1105` (`if node == NO_NODE || node >= 64 { return; }`) and the topology cache's `0..64` scan range. This is the SAME binding-side hazard items 100/101's F4/N8 already filed and left OPEN BY DESIGN (item 101's card, N8: "the binding-side hazard itself stays open BY DESIGN, exactly as N8 asked" — a deliberate deferral, not an oversight). Honestly documented at `src/lib.rs:465-470` and `README.md:158-161`. No new task filed for this half; it re-confirms the existing F4/N8 residual is still live and still correctly tracked as owner-deferred.
      - **Release-process blockers (BOTH already in flight, not new):** version `0.1.0`/`## Unreleased` — matches F1/#1262, already the next TaskList step per items 103/104's own recommended order; no CI result for the exact HEAD reviewed (`58d94da`) — this audit ran BEFORE task #1302's own push, so by construction no CI existed yet for that SHA; the push happened in this same session, immediately after (see the Evidence line below for the resulting CI run id).
      - **P1/P2 — NUMA gate residual risk restated:** Phases 2/4 waived (matches #1290's already-recorded decision, correctly characterized here as "принятое владельцем исключение, не техническое закрытие риска" / an accepted owner exception, not a technical risk closure — no disagreement with #1290's framing, a restatement of it). Phase 1's final re-run is the same already-tracked U1/U5/U6 requirement folded into #1262.
      - **P2 (NEW, CONFIRMED) — Windows `VirtualAllocExNuma(MEM_COMMIT)` base-pointer match is checked only by `debug_assert_eq!`, compiled out in release builds:** `src/lib.rs:1434` — **personally re-read**: `debug_assert_eq!(committed.cast::<u8>(), base, "VirtualAllocExNuma(MEM_COMMIT) must return the requested base for an already-reserved region")`, followed immediately by `Reservation::from_raw_parts(base, ...)`'s SAFETY comment explicitly relying on "Win32 contract: `committed == base`... verified by debug_assert above." In a release build this assertion is a no-op — if the Win32 API assumption is ever violated, `from_raw_parts` proceeds with a mismatched `base`, constructing a `Reservation` whose bookkeeping does not match what was actually committed. Not a confirmed live bug (the Win32 contract is well-established and no violation is known), but a load-bearing FFI assumption checked only in debug builds is exactly the hardening class CLAUDE.md's verification-first principle targets — release builds should fail closed (release the reservation, return `None`) rather than trust an unchecked assumption.
      - **P2 (already decided, restated as an alternate opinion, not adopted) — `NodeResolution::Resolved(u32)`'s lack of field-level `#[non_exhaustive]`:** this audit recommends the OPPOSITE of task #1298's just-landed decision ("до первой публикации API ещё можно сделать вариант non-exhaustive или заменить его на отдельную struct" / before the first publication the API can still be made non-exhaustive or replaced with a struct). Item 104 (the thirteenth review) independently re-derived and confirmed #1298's reasoning sound just one review cycle ago: the precedent (task #778/F13 for the architecturally-identical `MockCall::CurrentNode(u32)`) is real, already extended once, and the outcome is the conservative no-op that preserves `assert_eq!`-based downstream construction. **Not re-opened by this filing** — a fresh independent opinion against an already-considered-and-affirmed precedent is recorded here for the trail, not treated as new information requiring another owner round; if the owner wants to revisit it, that is their call, not something this review forces.
      - **P2 (already tracked, not new) — cold-path stack/syscall cost:** `Topology`'s `[u8; 1024] × 64` layout and up to 64 `open`/`read`/`close` triples on first `current_node()`. Matches the 5 unmeasured perf observations item 100 already filed to `docs/perf/OPEN_ITEMS.md` item 59 (task #1271) — same mechanism, no new information.
      - **P2 (already tracked, not new) — `#[doc(hidden)] pub` is not real privacy:** matches F5's already-decided (task #1289) semver-exempt-documentation policy; this audit correctly characterizes it as "допустимая зафиксированная политика, не фактическая граница API" (an accepted recorded policy, not an actual API boundary) — agreement with, not a challenge to, the already-landed decision.
      - **P3 (NEW, small, confirmed) — `mock::set_current_node`'s doc says the scripted value is returned by "the next call," but `CURRENT_NODE_SLOT` is not reset:** `src/lib.rs:227-230` — the scripted value is actually returned by ALL subsequent calls until the next `set_current_node`, not just the immediately-following one. One-word-class doc fix: "next call" → "subsequent calls until changed".
    - **Why filed instead of fixed here:** task #1302 is index-only per the item-89/90 class convention — the report and this card land together, before any finding is acted on. The one NEW, CONFIRMED P1 (`bind_range` page-alignment) requires an owner decision among three contract options the report itself lays out (require page-aligned `base` on Linux + fix the README example; accept a page envelope with an explicit caller contract; or surface the syscall's error/status) — matching this campaign's established pattern for API-contract-affecting decisions (F2/F5), this is NOT unilaterally implemented by the filing task. The P2 Windows debug_assert hardening and the P3 mock-doc wording are small, non-contract-changing fixes suitable for direct implementation without an owner round.
    - **Next trigger:** (1) owner decision on `bind_range`'s Linux contract (three options above) — the report's own recommended "naiboleye bezopasnyy malyy variant" (safest small option) is requiring page-aligned `base` and replacing the README's plain-`Vec` example with a page-aligned reservation, but this is presented as a recommendation, not a unilateral pick; (2) implement whichever option the owner selects, with a real regression test that would have caught this (not just "doesn't panic" — an oracle that can distinguish "bound" from "silently not bound," which likely requires either a scripted/mocked assertion of the syscall's arguments, or accepting this is fundamentally unverifiable without root/CAP_SYS_NICE and a real multi-node kernel, in which case the test should at minimum assert page-alignment of the address passed to the real mbind call site as a proxy); (3) the P2 Windows debug_assert hardening (small, no owner decision needed — release-fail-closed is the obviously correct direction); (4) the P3 mock-doc wording fix; (5) then resume the already-in-flight #1262 sequence (items 103/104's own recommended order), now informed by whatever `bind_range` fix landed.
    - **Evidence:** the report itself (`docs/reviews/2026-08-24-113155-numa-shim-publication-audit-Sol-codex.md`, committed by this task); `mbind(2)`'s ERRORS section fetched live from `https://man7.org/linux/man-pages/man2/mbind.2.html` by this filing task (not assumed from the report's citation alone) — confirms "EINVAL... addr is not a multiple of the system page size," with no rounding-down behavior; `crates/numa-shim/README.md:48-53` (the plain-`Vec` example, personally re-read); `crates/numa-shim/src/lib.rs:461-463,1120-1131` (the silently-discarded `mbind` return, personally re-read); `:1434` (the Windows `debug_assert_eq!`, personally re-read); `:227-230` (the mock doc wording, personally re-read); `docs/reviews/2026-08-24-102434-numa-shim-publication-readiness-review-oh.md` (item 104, T13's independent re-confirmation, cited for why the non_exhaustive counter-opinion is not re-opened here).
    - **Independence and mode, stated so this card does not overclaim.** The audit ran read-only, one agent, no sub-agents, no file edited, no git write command run, no `cargo` build/test run — a purely static, source-and-man-page read. This filing task went further than a transcription: it independently fetched and verified the `mbind(2)` alignment/EINVAL claim from the authoritative man page (the single fact the entire P1 finding rests on) rather than trusting the report's citation, and personally re-read every file:line citation for the two NEW findings (the `bind_range` gap and the Windows `debug_assert_eq!`) before filing them here.
    - **[Added 2026-08-24, task #1306 — the P1 `bind_range` finding: CLOSED, by removal.]** A follow-up analysis (the orchestrator, prompted by this item's own finding) surfaced a fact neither `@fh` nor `@ox`'s earlier consultations had caught: `mbind(2)`'s default flags (`0`, no `MPOL_MF_MOVE`) affect only FUTURE page faults, not pages already touched — so `vec![0u8; 4096]`'s pages, already faulted in by the `vec!` macro's zero-initialization before `bind_range` is ever called, could never have been retroactively placed even by a "successful" page-envelope fix. This made the three originally-framed options ((a) require alignment, (b) page envelope, (c) surface status) all inadequate on their own — confirmed by a fifth-option recommendation from an independent Sol-codex consultation, `docs/NUMA_BIND_RANGE_CONTRACT_RECOMMENDATION_2026-08-24-121245-Sol-codex.md`: remove the byte-range API entirely, replace it with a reservation-time-only API where "successfully bound" is a true statement by construction. Owner confirmed this direction explicitly. Implemented by task #1306, `2ffc9fc`, zero-trust reviewed personally (core `mbind`/`VirtualAllocExNuma` logic, the root crate's `bind_segment` removal and `reserve_aligned_on_node` rewiring, the unsafe-seam-inventory updates verified against the self-verifying `grep -rnE '^\s*#!?\[allow\(unsafe_code\)\]'` command, and the rewritten `tests/smoke.rs`/`tests/mock_dispatch.rs`/`tests/numa_seam.rs`), then independently re-executed (not just re-read) by this filing task: `cargo fmt --check`, `cargo clippy -p numa-shim --all-features --all-targets -- -D warnings`, `cargo test -p numa-shim --features vmem-integration` (5/5, real backend), `RUSTFLAGS="--cfg numa_shim_mock" cargo test -p numa-shim --features vmem-integration` (17+7+5+5, all passing), and the root crate's `cargo test --test numa_seam --features "numa-aware internals"` (4/4) — all GREEN, independently confirmed rather than trusted from the implementing task's own report. `bind_range` is gone; `reserve_on_node` is renamed `reserve_preferred_on_node` and returns `Result<Reservation, ReserveNumaError>` instead of `Option<Reservation>`; `NodeId` replaces raw `u32` node parameters in the reservation API; the Linux `node >= 64` and unknown-`SYS_MBIND`-architecture cases are now typed errors instead of silent no-ops; the root crate's test-only `bind_segment` unsafe seam is removed, and `reserve_aligned_on_node` (the real production caller) now composes its own best-effort fallback visibly at the call site instead of relying on a hidden fallback inside the shim. All breaking changes recorded in `crates/numa-shim/CHANGELOG.md` under `## Unreleased` (undated — task #1262's job). **This finding is CLOSED. No further action needed on it** — the crate's binding-side headline API is now honest by construction rather than merely better-documented.

F5 and F10 are EXECUTED by task #1310 (2026-08-24): the per-node raw-text cpumap cache was replaced by an allocation-free reverse index built once at init (`crate::cpumap::ReverseIndex`), F5's false per-node buffer rationale was rewritten with the correct global-CPU-ID-space derivation (`MAX_INDEXED_CPUS = 8192` from kernel `NR_CPUS` ceilings; init scratch buffer 4096), and both are covered by the new cross-platform `crates/numa-shim/tests/cpumap_reverse_index.rs` (6 tests, incl. the exact multi-node sparse-global-ID F5 scenario and the beyond-capacity degradation parity). **F1 — the review's only P1 — is EXECUTED by task #1308 (2026-08-24):** owner chose fail-closed remapping (via `AskUserQuestion`) over the other two options; `cpu_to_numa_node`'s `.unwrap_or(0)` fallback became `.unwrap_or(NO_NODE)` (the entire behavioral fix, one line — `current_node()`'s existing sentinel-to-`Option` wrapper handles the rest unchanged), `NodeResolution::FellBackToZero` renamed to `TopologyUnavailable` (the old name described a fallback that no longer exists), and every doc comment describing `current_node()`/`current_node_resolution()`/`NodeResolution` rewritten to the new honest semantics. Root crate's `src/alloc_core/numa.rs` needed ZERO changes (its existing `NO_NODE`-as-no-preference composed-fallback pattern from task #1306 already does the right thing automatically) — verified via `tests/numa_seam.rs` (4/4, unmodified, still green). **F4 is EXECUTED by task #1309 (2026-08-24):** `NodeId::new(u32) -> Self` (unchecked) became `NodeId::new(u32) -> Option<Self>`, returning `None` for exactly `NO_NODE` and `Some(_)` for every other `u32` — deliberately not a general node-existence validator, only closing the sentinel-representability gap the finding named; platform validity still lives at `reserve_preferred_on_node`. Every call site across the workspace updated (`src/alloc_core/numa.rs`, README, CHANGELOG, tests, bench); new `tests/node_id.rs` is the regression oracle. Incidentally, while independently verifying #1309's root-crate impact, discovered a genuinely pre-existing, unrelated red: `cargo clippy --features "numa-aware internals" -- -D warnings` fails on `main` with 6 lint errors no CI row exercises — filed as item 107 in `TRACKED_ci_gate_coverage.md`, not blocking this item. **F3+F6+F8 are EXECUTED by task #1311 (2026-08-24):** F8 — the false "8x repeat OOMs quickly" leak claim in `tests/smoke.rs` replaced with a real release oracle (Windows `VirtualQuery`, Linux `/proc/self/maps` scan, four probes spanning the whole reservation); the fragile `current_node().unwrap_or(0)` pattern replaced with loud self-skip when detection returns `None` across all three positive-NUMA-policy tests; `current_node_returns_valid_or_none`'s `node < 64` bound made platform-conditional (Linux-only, `<= u16::MAX` on Windows) instead of universal. F8's 4th sub-point (Windows `MemoryBasicInformation` `_WIN64`-only layout) deliberately left untouched, per F11/task #1313's still-open scope. F6 — the mock gained `mock::set_policy_failure`/`clear_policy_failure` (one-shot, node-scoped) plus new `MockCall::InstallPolicy`/`PolicyFailureRelease` variants, making the two-stage reserve-then-policy contract observable (reservation succeeded + policy failed; errno preserved; released exactly once) — a genuinely additive, breaking-on-the-success-path change (a successful call now records 2 entries, not 1); doc-honesty note added that the mock approximates Linux specifically, not Windows/macOS. F3 — new `crates/numa-shim/tests/policy_oracle_linux.rs`: a Linux-only oracle querying `get_mempolicy(2)`/`MPOL_F_ADDR` on a live reservation to prove the kernel's OWN record shows `MPOL_PREFERRED` for the requested node (not just trusting `reserve_preferred_on_node`'s `Ok` return), with a negative control proving the oracle isn't vacuous; cross-compile-checked only (dev host is Windows) on both `x86_64-unknown-linux-gnu` and `aarch64-unknown-linux-gnu`, first real execution pending a Linux CI run. **F7+F12 are EXECUTED by task #1312 (2026-08-24):** F7a — the self-contradiction in `reserve_preferred_on_node`'s doc comment (reservation time is "the only point where 'successfully bound' is a true statement" sitting four lines above "success does not guarantee physical placement") reconciled into one honest paragraph distinguishing "policy installed" from "placement guaranteed"; "binding"/"bound" language replaced with "preference"/"policy installation" language throughout the doc comments describing the actual contract, kept only for the historical `bind_range` API name and unavoidable subject-matter phrasing. F7b — the README's "no bind-existing-allocation API" paragraph no longer implies this is an OS-level impossibility ("would be a silent no-op lie"); corrected to state Linux `mbind(2)` CAN migrate resident pages via `MPOL_MF_MOVE`/`MPOL_MF_MOVE_ALL`, and that not implementing that is a deliberate crate-scope decision (a different, riskier, partially-failing contract), not a kernel limitation. F12 — the README's claim that a direct `aligned-vmem` dependency is needed to name `Reservation` corrected: `numa_shim::Reservation` is directly nameable via the crate's own `pub use aligned_vmem::Reservation` re-export (`lib.rs:209`); the direct dependency is genuinely needed only for `aligned-vmem`'s own items. The README's public-API summary block was also missing that re-export entry entirely — added. **F9+F11 are EXECUTED by task #1313 (2026-08-24), the final task of this wave:** F11 — owner decision (asked via `AskUserQuestion`, answered): numa-shim officially supports only 64-bit Windows; documentation-only across README/`lib.rs`'s platform matrix/`Cargo.toml`/the `smoke.rs` `PartitionId` comment, no code gate, no behavior change. F9 (safe half only) — `reserve_aligned_numa`'s aligned-base derivation now uses strict-provenance `.addr()`/`.with_addr()` instead of the `raw as usize -> base_u as *mut u8` integer round-trip, mirroring sibling `aligned-vmem`'s own equivalent fix; byte-identical addresses verified directly on this Windows host (not cross-compile-only) via the existing `VirtualQuery`-based over-reservation test. F9's other half — a one-call `MEM_RESERVE | MEM_COMMIT` fast path — deliberately NOT implemented (needs real measurement first per this repo's evidence discipline); filed as an explicitly unmeasured candidate, `docs/perf/OPEN_ITEMS.md` item 60. **All 12 findings from this review are now closed except F2** (the 0.2.0 version bump), which remains deliberately owner-scheduled, not a gap — see this card's own dedicated note above.
    - **Current verdict:** NO-GO on revision `8394108`. The report explicitly states the new reservation path is "close to publishable and noticeably more reliable than the previous version," and that **no confirmed UB, UAF, double-free or mistaken ownership transfer was found in the new Linux/Windows FFI branches** — the NO-GO rests on a semantic defect (F1), release metadata (F2), and missing verification evidence (F3), not on memory-safety.
    - **The twelve findings, with this filing task's own verification verdict per finding.** Six were personally re-verified against the source before filing (not transcribed): **F1 (P1, CONFIRMED)** — `current_node()` maps `NodeResolution::FellBackToZero` to `Some(0)`, so an unreadable/truncated/`>= 64`-node Linux topology becomes an authoritative-looking node 0; the new `reserve_preferred_on_node` then successfully installs a preference for the WRONG node and returns `Ok`, i.e. the fail-closed policy API works correctly but on a wrong input. Verified at `crates/numa-shim/src/lib.rs:431-441` (the documented `FellBackToZero -> Some(0)` mapping table) and `:485-500` (the crate's own doc already admitting `Some(0)` "does not guarantee the calling thread is actually on node 0"); the in-tree consumer path `src/alloc_core/numa.rs:34-35,76-86` does exactly this. The report's recommended fix is fail-closed remapping (`FellBackToZero -> None`) plus preferring `getcpu(2)` over the sysfs cache. **F4 (P2, CONFIRMED)** — `NodeId::new` is an unchecked `const fn` accepting any `u32` (`lib.rs:86-119`) while its own doc at `:98-100` states `NO_NODE` "must NOT be wrapped"; the type does not make the forbidden state unrepresentable. **F5 (P2, CONFIRMED)** — `NODE_CPUMAP_BUF_LEN = 1024`'s justification comment (`lib.rs:953-971`) reasons in "CPUs on a SINGLE node," but a Linux node cpumap is a GLOBAL cpumask whose bit index is the global logical CPU ID, so the buffer bounds global CPU-ID space, not per-node CPU count; the comment's "far below" claim is arithmetically wrong. Practical exposure is low today but the stated rationale is false, and an overflow degrades into F1's silent node-0 fallback. **F7 (P2 docs, CONFIRMED)** — `lib.rs:545-551` calls reservation time "the only point where successfully bound is a true statement" and `:553-554` immediately says `MPOL_PREFERRED` is a soft preference that "does not guarantee physical placement"; the two sit four lines apart. **F8 (P2/P3, CONFIRMED for its first sub-point)** — `tests/smoke.rs:144-153`'s comment claims the 8-iteration repeat loop "OOMs quickly" if `Drop` frees only `span` instead of `reservation_len`; 8 × 8 MiB is ~64 MiB of virtual address space with almost no physical commit, which a 64-bit process will not OOM on — the stated leak oracle does not hold. **F12 (P3 docs, CONFIRMED)** — `README.md:65-70` says a direct `aligned-vmem` dependency is needed to name the return type, but `lib.rs:173-181` deliberately `pub use aligned_vmem::Reservation` for exactly that purpose; the direct dependency is genuinely needed for `page_size`/`PAGE`/the explicit fallback, not for the type name. The remaining findings were NOT independently re-verified by this filing task and are filed as the report's claims: **F2** (release metadata: version still `0.1.0`, `## Unreleased` CHANGELOG, README/root pins at `0.1`, waiver SHA unfilled), **F3** (no independent oracle that a successful `mbind` actually installed the VMA policy — the smoke test treats `Ok` as its own proof; plus incomplete real-NUMA gate phases), **F6** (the mock cannot script policy-failure-after-successful-reservation, and diverges from the real backends on Windows `node >= 64` and macOS), **F9** (Windows always does reserve+commit as two calls with an over-reserve, where a single `MEM_RESERVE | MEM_COMMIT` fast path is available for `align <= allocation granularity`, as the sibling `aligned-vmem` already implements), **F10** (Linux hot lookup remains O(nodes × cpumap bytes) over a ~64 KiB cache), **F11** (32-bit Windows support is undefined: the platform matrix says "Windows," the test FFI layout is hardcoded 64-bit, no i686 CI row).
    - **F2 deliberately NOT filed as a task — explicit owner instruction.** The owner instructed, in the same message that delivered this review, to ignore the version-bump item ("игнорируем бап версии... я сам скажу когда это сделать") and deleted the pre-existing task #1262 that tracked it. F2 remains a genuine, correctly-identified release blocker and is recorded here in the index so it is not lost, but it is owner-scheduled, not agent-scheduled: no task exists for it and none should be created without an explicit instruction.
    - **Next trigger:** (1) F1 — DONE via task #1308 (fail-closed remapping, owner-chosen via `AskUserQuestion`); (2) F4 — DONE via task #1309 (`NodeId::new` valid-by-construction w.r.t. `NO_NODE`); (3) F5/F10 together — DONE via task #1310 (the reverse-index option was implemented; `getcpu(2)` remains an unmeasured future candidate tracked in `docs/perf/OPEN_ITEMS.md` item 59); (4) F3/F6/F8 — DONE via task #1311 (real release oracle, self-skip discipline, platform-conditional bound; scripted two-stage mock; Linux-only `get_mempolicy` policy oracle — F3's first real execution still pending a Linux CI run); (5) F7/F12 — DONE via task #1312 (soft-preference wording reconciled; Reservation re-export claim corrected); (6) F9/F11 — DONE via task #1313 (strict-provenance refactor, 64-bit-only Windows policy decision; the one-call fast path deferred as an unmeasured `docs/perf/OPEN_ITEMS.md` candidate, item 60); (7) F2 — owner-scheduled only, the sole remaining item.
    - **Evidence:** the report itself (`docs/reviews/2026-08-24-170047-numa-shim-publication-audit-Sol-codex.md`, committed by this task); the six file:line citations personally re-read and confirmed by this filing task, listed per-finding above; CI run `32741635275` on the reviewed revision `8394108` (the report notes it was still `in_progress` with 0 failed jobs at snapshot time — this filing task tracks its final outcome separately under task #1307, which is what produced `8394108` in the first place).

The wave that closed this review's five P1/P2 findings ran as four sequential `/crush` + worktree tasks (#1318-#1321), each personally zero-trust reviewed (full diff read, independent re-run of the exact failing/passing verification commands, never trusting the agent's own report) before merging, plus one incidental same-day CI-red fix (task #1323, unrelated to this review's own findings, described above) discovered and fixed mid-wave when `origin/main`'s real CI (not just local checks) was confirmed red.
    - **The findings, with this filing task's own verification verdict per finding — ALL FIVE P1/P2 personally re-verified against source before filing (not transcribed):**
      - **P1 (CONFIRMED) — the Linux policy oracle skips on ANY `ReserveNumaError::Os`:** `crates/numa-shim/tests/policy_oracle_linux.rs:190-202` — personally re-read: the `Err(ReserveNumaError::Os(e))` arm unconditionally `eprintln!`-skips and returns, with a comment naming only the legitimate cgroup-restricted case. But `Os` also carries implementation errors — `EINVAL` (bad addr/len/maxnode marshalling), `EFAULT`, `ENOSYS` (wrong syscall number on a new arch) — so a regression that makes `mbind` ALWAYS fail leaves CI green via skip. The oracle built by task #1311 to prove the flagship API works can therefore vacuously pass exactly when the API is broken. Fix: split expected environment errno (skip, allowlisted) from implementation errno (hard fail — at minimum `EINVAL`/`EFAULT`/`ENOSYS`), print the errno in the skip diagnostic.
      - **P2 (CONFIRMED) — `EINTR` during first topology load permanently disables NUMA detection:** `crates/numa-shim/src/lib.rs:1452-1493` (`read_cpumap_into`) — personally re-read: any `read()` returning `-1` closes the fd and returns `None` with NO errno check; the caller then commits the incomplete `ReverseIndex` into a `OnceLock` for the process's whole lifetime. One signal delivered during the first `current_node()` call turns a healthy topology into a permanent `None` (fail-closed, so not unsafe — but a real availability defect). Fix: capture errno before `close()`, retry `read` (and `open`) on `EINTR` with a bounded retry count; all other errno keep today's fail-closed behavior.
      - **P2 (CONFIRMED) — the policy oracle itself contains an integer pointer round-trip:** `tests/policy_oracle_linux.rs:215` (`unsafe { r.as_ptr().add(page) } as usize`) and `:148` (`addr as *mut core::ffi::c_void` inside `get_mempolicy_addr`) — personally re-read. The same strict-provenance discipline task #1313 just applied to the Windows production path is violated by the test written in the same wave. Test-only, no production exposure, but the inconsistency is real. Fix: pass the raw pointer through unchanged (`*mut c_void` parameter), no usize intermediary.
      - **P2 (CONFIRMED) — mock test leaves a scripted policy failure armed in thread-local state:** `crates/numa-shim/tests/mock_dispatch.rs:280-330` (`policy_failure_script_for_other_node_does_not_fire`) — personally re-read: the test arms node 5's failure slot, exercises node 3, and ends (`drop(r2)`) WITHOUT `clear_policy_failure()`. `POLICY_FAILURE_SLOT` is thread-local, and the test harness reuses worker threads, so a later test on the same thread that touches node 5 inherits the armed failure — currently masked only by neighboring tests defensively clearing state at START, an implicit ordering dependency. Fix: explicit cleanup at test end (guard or trailing `clear_policy_failure()`), removing the ordering dependence.
      - **P2 (CONFIRMED) — 64-bit-Windows-only remains documentation-only with no compile gate:** `crates/numa-shim/Cargo.toml:15-24` — personally re-read: the comment itself says "Documentation-only policy — no `cfg(target_pointer_width)` gate is enforced"; `grep compile_error\|target_pointer_width src/lib.rs` confirms only doc-comment mentions, no gate. A 32-bit Windows user gets a compiling artifact whose test FFI layout (`MEMORY_BASIC_INFORMATION` in `tests/smoke.rs`) hardcodes `_WIN64` — false compatibility confidence. The review recommends the enforceable variant of the SAME policy the owner already chose in task #1313/F11: a `compile_error!` for `all(windows, target_pointer_width = "32")`. This ENFORCES, not reverses, the owner's 64-bit-only decision.
      - **P3 (already tracked, not new) — Windows one-call `MEM_RESERVE | MEM_COMMIT` fast path:** unmeasured; `docs/perf/OPEN_ITEMS.md` item 60, filed by task #1313. The review agrees it is not a correctness blocker and must be A/B-measured first.
      - **P3 (already tracked, not new) — reverse-index cold start (up to 64 `open`/`read`/`close` on first call) and `getcpu(2)` comparison unmeasured:** matches `docs/perf/OPEN_ITEMS.md` item 59's existing unmeasured candidates; the review explicitly endorses keeping the current reverse-index solution until numbers exist.
    - **Next trigger:** (1) P1 — DONE via task #1318 (errno-classified skip/fail split, `546e8d8`); (2) P2-EINTR — DONE via task #1319 (bounded retry, `652d505`); (3) P2-provenance + P2-mock-hygiene — DONE via task #1320 (`6f242f1`); (4) P2-32bit — DONE via task #1321 (`compile_error!` gate, `25e25e7`); (5) then the SEVENTEENTH independent readiness review on the resulting head (task #1322, ready to launch). P3s: no action until measured, per items 59/60.
    - **Evidence:** the report itself (committed by this task); the five file:line citations personally re-read and confirmed by this filing task, listed per-finding above; `crates/numa-shim/tests/policy_oracle_linux.rs:190-202,215,148`, `crates/numa-shim/src/lib.rs:1452-1493`, `crates/numa-shim/tests/mock_dispatch.rs:280-330`, `crates/numa-shim/Cargo.toml:15-24`, all re-read at revision `9137c51` (the reviewed revision, which is also the current `origin/main` head — no drift between review and verification).

Nodemask sizing enlarged from a bare `u64` to `[u64; 16]` (1024 nodes) with matching `maxnode = 1024`, fixing the >64-node-host false-oracle-failure (F6) — verified against the kernel's own `maxnode < nr_node_ids` pre-copy-out rejection and `CONFIG_NODES_SHIFT`'s Kconfig range on both x86_64 and aarch64. This REFINES (not reverses) task #1318's classification — #1318's core contribution (unconditional skip → SOME classification) stands; this task corrects which errno land in which bucket and adds the missing preflight. Real execution remains cross-compile-only pending Linux CI, the same limitation as every prior Linux-only oracle task in this campaign.
    - **The findings, with this filing task's own verification verdict per finding:**
      - **F1 (P1) — the policy oracle's task-#1318 errno classification is wrong in BOTH directions, and the detection-skip hole remains:** (1) **CONFIRMED against `mbind(2)`'s documented contract** — the kernel returns `EINVAL` not only for marshalling bugs but for a well-formed call whose nodemask contains no node that is online, allowed by the calling thread's cpuset, or memory-bearing; `current_node()` proves only that the CPU maps to the node, NOT that the node is in `cpuset_mems_allowed` or has memory — so on a CPU-only/memoryless node or a split cpuset, #1318's unconditional `EINVAL` panic is a FALSE RED on a legitimate environment. `ENOSYS` can likewise come from a seccomp/sandbox policy, not only a wrong syscall number. (2) The inverse arm is too wide: any OTHER `Some(errno)` (including `EBADF`/`ERANGE`/anything unexpected) silently skips green — an unknown implementation regression still hides. The review's fix: a PREFLIGHT probe via `get_mempolicy(MPOL_F_MEMS_ALLOWED)` to confirm the target node is allowed and memory-bearing before the positive call, then an errno ALLOWLIST (expected-environment errno explicitly enumerated and skipped; everything else — including unknown errno — fails), not "everything except three panics"/"everything else skips". (3) The `current_node() == None` green-skip hole — IDENTICAL to item 109's P2-1, already being fixed by task #1324 (in flight), which this review independently re-derives with the same env-flag strict-mode proposal.
      - **F2 (P2) — smoke.rs target-OS expectations vs mock backend:** converges with item 109's P2-2 (task #1325); this review's proposed mechanism differs (compile-gate the real-platform smoke tests `not(numa_shim_mock)` and keep mock-contract testing in `mock_dispatch.rs`, vs item 109's backend-aware `cfg!` branches) — the implementing task should weigh both.
      - **F3 (P2, CONFIRMED by this filing task against `src/lib.rs:1275-1283`)** — `current_node_impl` calls `sched_getcpu()` FIRST, then `cpu_to_numa_node`, whose first call runs the up-to-64-file topology scan inside `OnceLock::get_or_init` — so the first-ever call's CPU snapshot is taken BEFORE a long initialization window during which the thread can migrate, and the stale CPU is then looked up in the fresh topology. Same in `current_node_resolution_impl` (`:1285-1296`). Fix: touch `topology()` first, then take the CPU snapshot and do a direct lookup.
      - **F4 (P2) — `O_CLOEXEC`:** identical to item 109's P3-2 (task #1327); this review rates it P2 (allocator-adjacent library, real fd-inheritance defect) vs item 109's P3 — either way already tasked.
      - **F5 (P2, CONFIRMED by this filing task against `tests/node_resolution_linux.rs:41-45`)** — `current_node_agrees_with_resolution_mapping` calls `current_node()` and `current_node_resolution()` as two INDEPENDENT snapshots, each doing its own `sched_getcpu()`; on a real multi-node machine a scheduler migration between the two calls makes correct `Some(0)`/`Resolved(1)` a false failure. Single-node CI hides the race; the planned real-multi-NUMA gate would surface it. Fix: single-snapshot oracle via a test-only helper (the doc-hidden `linux::dbg_*` pattern already exists in this file), or pinned affinity.
      - **F6 (P3) — `maxnode=65` limits the oracle to hosts with `nr_node_ids <= 65`:** modern kernels reject `get_mempolicy` with `EINVAL` when `maxnode < nr_node_ids` BEFORE any copy-out, so on a >64-node host the oracle's own probe fails regardless of the target node being in 0..63. Not independently verified (kernel-source claim; plausible and consistent with the man page's maxnode wording). Fix: a larger fixed nodemask array with matching maxnode; check the one low bit needed.
      - **F7 (P3) — `ErrorKind::Interrupted` + testable predicate:** identical to item 109's P3-1 (task #1327).
      - **F8 (P3) — default-feature rustdoc gap:** identical to item 109's P2-3 (task #1326; already personally reproduced there).
      - **F9 (P3) — stale smoke.rs comment says 32-bit Windows policy "undecided":** matches the pre-existing-staleness observation task #1321's own zero-trust review already recorded in item 108's card (deliberately left untouched then as out-of-scope; now flagged by an independent review, so a task exists to fix it — folded into #1333).
      - **F10 (P3) — perf docs overclaim ("no syscalls" for warm `current_node()`, which still does `sched_getcpu` every call) and both mock-backend benches measure recording overhead, not real cold/warm cost; README's "zero C library dependencies" vs actual system-libc FFI wording** — doc/bench-honesty fixes, folded into #1333 (docs) with the bench-measurement half left to the perf backlog per items 59/60's measure-first discipline.
      - **F11 (P3) — `parse_each_set_cpu` is O(words²) on the cold path** (per-word `nth_token` rescans from the start; `index_node` does the pass twice; topology init repeats × up to 64 nodes). Algorithmic-cost class, same family as the F10/#1310 reverse-index fix. Fix: linear right-to-left iteration (`rsplit`) with an incrementing word index, keeping the two-pass fail-closed structure; confirm with a real-parser benchmark, not a mock bench (task #1334).
    - **F3 EXECUTED by task #1331 (2026-08-25, `5f14b3d`):** Linux `current_node_impl`/`current_node_resolution_impl` now call `topology()` BEFORE `sched_getcpu()`, reusing the returned reference for a direct `.lookup()` instead of going through `cpu_to_numa_node`/`cpu_to_numa_node_checked` (left untouched — still used by the doc-hidden test-only `dbg_*` forwarders). On the first-ever call, the CPU snapshot is now taken AFTER the up-to-64-file sysfs topology scan completes, so the scan can no longer widen the migration window between snapshot and lookup; warm calls are unaffected (same single `OnceLock` check either way). Minimal reorder only — the review's optional extra-strictness suggestion (re-snapshot and retry once if the CPU changed) deliberately NOT implemented (the review itself marks it optional). Production code, Linux-only; no version bump. The migration-window narrowing itself is not deterministically testable (no portable way to force a mid-scan scheduler migration) — verified by code reading plus existing tests staying green.
    - **F5 EXECUTED by task #1332 (2026-08-25, `55d7c93`):** `tests/node_resolution_linux.rs`'s `current_node_agrees_with_resolution_mapping` no longer calls `current_node()`/`current_node_resolution()` as two independent (and thus independently-migratable) snapshots — it now takes ONE raw `sched_getcpu()` sample (self-contained `extern "C"` declaration inside the test file, matching `policy_oracle_linux.rs`'s own established raw-syscall pattern) and feeds that SAME value into the two pre-existing doc-hidden `linux::dbg_current_node_for_cpu`/`dbg_node_resolution_for_cpu` forwarders — neither of which re-samples the CPU, so no scheduler migration can occur between them. `src/lib.rs` completely untouched (no new production surface, so no risk of the dbg-hook-allowlist-omission defect class that hit this campaign twice before, tasks #1287/#1315). No wall-clock speedup measured or claimed — `fix(perf)` commit prefix per this repo's own taxonomy, matching task #1310's own precedent for an unmeasured complexity-only win; the O(w²)→O(w) reduction is provable by construction (single pass), not benchmarked. Byte-for-byte behavior preservation verified by explicit edge-case analysis in the CHANGELOG entry (empty input, trailing/leading/double comma, word-order boundary) against the OLD `nth_token`-based semantics. No version bump.
