      - **P2-1 (CONFIRMED) — the new `MPOL_F_MEMS_ALLOWED` preflight (task #1329) hard-panics in exactly the seccomp/container environment the file's own `ERRNO_EPERM` allowlist entry exists to tolerate:** `crates/numa-shim/tests/policy_oracle_linux.rs:388-395` (the bare `.expect` on `get_mems_allowed()`) against `:193-199` (`ERRNO_EPERM`'s doc, naming Docker's default seccomp profile denying `mbind` with EPERM as "the F8.2 container case this skip arm has always existed for") and `:432-441` (the `Some(ERRNO_EPERM) => skip` arm, unreachable in that same environment because Docker's seccomp profile denies `get_mempolicy`/`mbind`/`set_mempolicy` as one group). Personally re-read all three cited spans: confirmed the preflight has no errno classification at all — any error from `get_mems_allowed()`, including EPERM/ENOSYS, panics via `.expect` before the reserve call's own (correct) allowlist is ever reached. Net effect: `cargo test -p numa-shim --features vmem-integration` goes hard red on containerized Linux. Fix: classify the preflight's errno with the same allowlist the reserve arm already has (EPERM/ENOSYS ⇒ skip, else panic).
      - **P2-2 (CONFIRMED) — `tests/smoke.rs`'s platform predicate is arch-blind: four tests hard-fail on every Linux target that is not x86_64/aarch64:** `crates/numa-shim/tests/smoke.rs:170-185,272-287,483-490,499-518` vs `src/lib.rs:1432-1436` (production dispatch, arch-gated) and `src/lib.rs:56`/`README.md:241-243` (the platform matrix documenting "Linux other arch (non-miri)" as a supported `UnsupportedArchitecture`-returning row). Personally re-read: the predicate is `cfg!(any(numa_shim_mock, all(any(target_os = "linux", windows), not(miri))))` — target-OS and backend only, never `target_arch`, while `tests/policy_oracle_linux.rs:45-51` (the sibling file) already carries the missing `any(target_arch = "x86_64", target_arch = "aarch64")` clause, and `src/lib.rs:1401,1432` confirm the production dispatch itself is arch-gated. On a real target such as `armv7-unknown-linux-gnueabihf`, four tests would panic/assert-fail expecting `Ok`/`InvalidArguments`/`InvalidNode` and instead getting `Err(UnsupportedArchitecture)`. This is the third dimension of a predicate defect already corrected twice (target-OS, then backend via task #1325's P2-2) — recurs because the predicate still classifies fewer axes than production dispatch does. Fix: three-way classification matching production (mock ⇒ success; real Linux/Windows x86_64/aarch64 ⇒ success; real Linux other-arch ⇒ `UnsupportedArchitecture`; else ⇒ `UnsupportedPlatform`).
      - **P2-3 (CONFIRMED) — the one CI row that executes the flagship policy oracle (`tests/policy_oracle_linux.rs`) carries no green-and-dead sentinel:** `.github/workflows/ci.yml:2706-2708` (the `NUMA_SHIM_REQUIRE_ORACLE=1` row). Personally re-read: `policy_oracle_linux.rs` is `#![cfg(...)]`-gated on five simultaneous conditions; if any one silently stops holding (feature rename, stray cfg leak, arch-clause edit, `#![cfg]` typo), the file compiles to zero tests and the row still exits 0 — CI reports green with the crate's headline `mbind`-installs-`MPOL_PREFERRED` capability entirely unverified. Every *less* important numa-shim CI row already `grep -F`s specific `... ok` sentinels (`:2664-2666`, `:2674-2675`, macOS row `:2855-2859`); this one, the most important, has none. `NUMA_SHIM_REQUIRE_ORACLE=1` (task #1324) closes the silent-*skip* hole but is powerless against the never-*compiled* hole. Fix: `tee` the test output and `grep -F` the two positive-oracle test names.
      - **P3-1 (already reasoned sound, not independently re-derived beyond the review's own UAPI citation) — `O_CLOEXEC = 0o2000000` (task #1327) is applied unconditionally on every Linux arch, but the value is sparc/sparc64 (`0x400000`, this bit is undefined there) and alpha/parisc (`010000000`, this bit means `O_DIRECT` there) specific, not universal:** `src/lib.rs:1569,1608,1336`. The doc comment honestly scopes the verification to x86_64/aarch64; the code's `cfg` gate does not match that scope.
      - **P3-2 (already reasoned sound) — the `/proc/self/maps` release-oracle comment's "unlikely to race" reasoning is inverted for Linux's actual top-down `mmap` placement policy** (a freed region is exactly where the NEXT `mmap` lands preferentially, not "somewhere in megabytes of slack"): `crates/numa-shim/tests/smoke.rs:419-423,427-454`. Not observed to fail; the finding is that the documented mitigation is not the actual mitigation.
      - **P3-3 (already reasoned sound) — the topology `OnceLock` initializer's peak stack frame (~12-20 KiB, an `8192`-byte `ReverseIndex` local plus a possible NRVO-missed move) is undocumented on the one path the crate advertises as global-allocator-reentrant-safe** (task #777's allocation-free rewrite analyzed heap use, not stack use): `src/lib.rs:1487-1523`.
      - **P3-4 (already reasoned sound) — the README's code examples are compiled by nothing** (no `include_str!`, no `tests/readme_*.rs`, no doc-drift guard script unlike sibling `aligned-vmem`), and the crate has already shipped one broken example once (F6/task #1268): `README.md:55-63,97-147,170-226`. The `## Public API` block is also ` ```rust `-fenced with bodiless signatures — not valid Rust, and inconsistent with this crate's own doc-comment convention of ` ```text ` for the same shape.
      - **P3-5 (already reasoned sound) — the still-open `## Unreleased` CHANGELOG section has a duplicate `### Changed` heading (the same class task #1274/N10 already fixed once) and describes API that no longer exists in the same unreleased cycle** (`FellBackToZero`, renamed to `TopologyUnavailable`; `bind_range`'s `node >= 64` no-op behavior, `bind_range` itself removed by task #1306): `crates/numa-shim/CHANGELOG.md:57,169,128-137,139-144`.     - **The findings, with this filing task's own verification verdict per finding — BOTH P2s personally re-verified against source before filing (not transcribed):**
      - **F1 (P2, CONFIRMED) — `O_CLOEXEC` is DISABLED (not corrected) on sparc/sparc64, when the correct UAPI value is already known and documented right next to the disabling code:** `crates/numa-shim/src/lib.rs:1565-1598`. Personally re-read: task #1339 (nineteenth review P3-1) split the constant so sparc/sparc64 compile to `const O_CLOEXEC: core::ffi::c_int = 0;` (no close-on-exec at all) rather than sparc's actual UAPI value — and the doc comment directly above states that value explicitly: "sparc's UAPI defines `O_CLOEXEC` as `0x400000`". Task #1339's own reasoning (recorded in this same card, item 111, P3-1) was to avoid "passing an unrelated bit to `open(2)`" — a sound principle when the correct value is UNKNOWN, but the correct value IS known and already written in the comment, so disabling the hardening entirely leaves a real (if narrow) fork+exec fd-leak race on a real rustc target (`sparc64-unknown-linux-gnu`) that a one-line fix removes. Fix: `#[cfg(any(target_arch = "sparc", target_arch = "sparc64"))] const O_CLOEXEC: core::ffi::c_int = 0x400000;` instead of `0`.
      - **F2 (P2, CONFIRMED) — the crate's own public examples (`README.md` and the `NodeId::new` rustdoc snippet) can PANIC on a real Linux architecture where detection succeeds but the reservation backend does not:** `crates/numa-shim/README.md:97-123`, `crates/numa-shim/src/lib.rs:140-157` (the "Ergonomic path from detection" section fixed by task #1341), and their compiled oracle copies in `crates/numa-shim/tests/readme_examples.rs:64-76,190-201`. Personally re-read all four sites: on a real Linux target outside x86_64/aarch64 (e.g. `riscv64gc-unknown-linux-gnu`, `s390x-unknown-linux-gnu`, `powerpc64le-unknown-linux-gnu` — all real, actively-used rustc targets), `current_node()`'s detection chain (`sched_getcpu` + sysfs cpumap) is architecture-independent and can genuinely resolve `Some(node)`, while `reserve_preferred_on_node` returns `Err(ReserveNumaError::UnsupportedArchitecture)` because the `mbind`-based reservation path is x86_64/aarch64-only. Both the README's first example and `NodeId::new`'s "Ergonomic path" rustdoc snippet call `.expect("NUMA-preferred reservation failed")` on that `Result` — a downstream user on one of those architectures who copies the example verbatim gets a panic, not the demonstrated behavior. `tests/readme_examples.rs`'s own module doc (added by task #1341) ALREADY documents this exact panic path as "a known property rather than a surprise" — but a documented panic risk in a copy-pasted public example is still a real risk, not a resolved one. 119. **[T, FIRST independent publication audit of `racy-ptr-cell`] `docs/reviews/2026-08-25-150109-racy-ptr-cell-publication-audit-run-1-Sol-codex.md`** (author Sol-codex, self-titled "прогон 1"; reported 2026-08-25 15:01:09 +02:00; verdict **CONDITIONAL NO-GO — production algorithm correctness confirmed clean from scratch (atomic `null → sentinel → real pointer` protocol, Release/Acquire publication, rollback on `None`/panic, loser re-race, sentinel-collision defense all consistent between code and the loom model; no P0/P1 memory/ownership/ordering defect), but three verification/documentation-honesty and integration-drift issues should close before publish; no algorithm change needed**) is a STATIC read-only audit (no sub-agents; tests/build/`cargo check`/Clippy/Miri/rustdoc/benchmarks/`cargo publish --dry-run` not run). Committed directly to `main` (`5fac85a`) by the reviewing process before this filing task read it — same unprompted-arrival pattern already established and confirmed benign for the numa-shim campaign's items 110/112/113/115/117/118, now observed on a second crate. (Filed 2026-08-25, this session.)
    - **Status:** CLOSED (2026-08-25, same session) — all three P1/P2 findings EXECUTED: F1 by task #1358 (`9c49337`), F2 by task #1359 (`29c2b2f`), F3 by task #1360 (`7e20a4a`). Two P3 findings (F4/F5) remain explicitly deferred by the review itself to "the next API/perf pass"; two non-blocking metadata decisions (name, description length) converge with already-tracked item 28 above, unaffected by this wave.
    - **The findings, personally re-verified against source before filing — all three P1/P2 findings CONFIRMED, not transcribed:**
      - **F1 (P1, CONFIRMED) — a unit test's own comment claims strict-provenance cleanliness the crate's own commit history already admits is false:** `crates/racy-ptr-cell/tests/cell_unit.rs:91-117`. The test ferries a pointer's address across a channel as a `usize` (because `NonNull<T>` is `!Send`) and reconstructs it via `core::ptr::with_exposed_provenance_mut`, with a comment claiming this "keeps this test clean under `-Zmiri-strict-provenance`". Personally re-read the commit that introduced this exact comment, `ead400a` (task #774): its OWN commit message body says, verbatim, "the review's own suggested-close text claimed this would also pass under -Zmiri-strict-provenance, which is incorrect -- that flag forbids the exposed-provenance mechanism entirely, so no amount of expose_provenance()/with_exposed_provenance discipline can satisfy it. Verified both ways locally." — the commit message got this right; the code comment it landed alongside never did, and has been wrong ever since. Not a production UB risk (the affected code is test-only, ferrying an address that was never concurrently accessed as a raw int-derived pointer in production logic) — a verification-honesty defect: the test's own claim about what it proves is false. **EXECUTED by task #1358 (2026-08-25, `9c49337`):** the pointer is no longer transported through the channel at all — the worker thread sends back only a completion signal, and the receiving thread calls `cell.get()` directly for the `NonNull<Payload>`. Personally verified under BOTH permissive miri AND `MIRIFLAGS="-Zmiri-strict-provenance"` (both pass) — the strict-provenance run is positive proof the claim is now actually true, not merely no-longer-false.
      - **F2 (P2, CONFIRMED) — the root crate's `#[cfg(loom)] mod loom_shim` (a separate, hand-duplicated `RacyPtrCell` implementation needed because loom's real cell can't be a `const fn`) has drifted from the real crate's current guarantees, while its own doc comment still calls it "behaviourally faithful":** `src/registry/bootstrap.rs:225-341`. Personally read the shim in full: no `align_of::<T>() >= 2` check; no `RollbackGuard`/panic-unwind protection in `get_or_try_init` (a panicking `init()` closure would leave the sentinel wedged forever, with no rollback path at all — directly contradicting the module doc's "same ... rollback" claim); `dbg_rollback_reenterable`'s final restore is an UNCONDITIONAL `self.ptr.store(core::ptr::null_mut(), Ordering::Release)` with no check for a concurrent winner having published a real pointer in between — confirmed present verbatim, exactly the clobber risk the finding describes. The shim's existence is justified (a `const fn` constructor is required for the root static; the real crate's own `RacyPtrCell::new()` cannot be `const` while carrying the RollbackGuard/alignment machinery), but the drift itself is real and the "identical/behaviourally faithful" framing overclaims. Not a numa-shim/other-crate blocker — root-crate-internal, `#[cfg(loom)]`-only code with no external consumer — but a maintenance hazard: the shim could silently diverge further from the real cell's contract with no automated check to catch it. **EXECUTED by task #1359 (2026-08-25, `29c2b2f`):** all four properties added to the shim, mirroring the real crate exactly (RollbackGuard type, const-eval align assert, release-active sentinel-address check on `init()`'s return, and `dbg_rollback_reenterable`'s conditional restore keyed on its own postcondition CAS). Verified under both `RUSTFLAGS="--cfg loom"` and plain builds: `loom_thread_free` (compiles+passes, proving the const constructor still works for the `static REGISTRY`), and the dedicated `regression_bootstrap_oom_sentinel_rollback` test (which exercises `dbg_rollback_reenterable` directly) under both configurations.
      - **F3 (P2, CONFIRMED) — the module doc calls the loser spin-wait "bounded and short in practice", but nothing in the type signature or contract actually bounds it:** `crates/racy-ptr-cell/src/lib.rs:44-49`. Personally re-read: `get_or_try_init`'s `init` closure is arbitrary caller code (`FnMut() -> Option<NonNull<T>>` — no `Send`/no-block/no-syscall restriction of any kind); a loser thread spins on `core::hint::spin_loop()` until the winner's closure returns, so an `init` that blocks on a syscall, gets preempted, or simply runs long makes every loser's wait exactly as long — unbounded in the general case, "bounded" only under the unstated assumption that callers keep `init` fast. The doc DOES correctly forbid re-entering the same cell from within `init`, but states no general non-blocking/short-running contract. **EXECUTED by task #1360 (2026-08-25, `7e20a4a`):** the module doc's "spin-wait" section reworded to state plainly there is no bounded-latency guarantee, only a caller obligation to keep `init` fast; a matching sentence added to `get_or_try_init`'s own doc comment next to the existing re-entry restriction. Not renamed to "lock-free", per the review's own caution.
      - **F4 (P3, deferred by the review itself to "the next API/perf pass" — not filed as a task)** — `get_or_try_init` takes `FnMut` though the closure is called at most once per call (`src/lib.rs:371-373`); `FnOnce` would express the real contract and accept consuming closures without breaking existing `FnMut`/`Fn` callers.
      - **F5 (P3, deferred by the review itself — not filed as a task)** — the claim CAS's `Acquire` success ordering may be unnecessarily strong (a `Relaxed` success order is a candidate micro-optimization worth checking against the loom model separately); the existing benchmark measures cold-path cost entangled with `Box::leak`/allocator growth, not cell overhead in isolation, and has no contended/loser-spin scenario.
      - **Two metadata decisions restated, not duplicated — already tracked as item 28 above:** the crate name `racy-ptr-cell` could read as "has data races" (the opposite of its actual guarantee), and `Cargo.toml`'s `description` is long for crates.io search-result UI. This review's own framing ("Неблокирующие решения перед публикацией") matches item 28's existing disposition exactly; no new card needed.

121. **[T, THIRD independent publication audit of `racy-ptr-cell`, Sol-codex run 2] `docs/reviews/2026-08-25-164200-racy-ptr-cell-publication-audit-run-2-Sol-codex.md`** (author Sol-codex, self-titled "прогон 2"; reported 2026-08-25 16:42:00 +02:00; verdict **условный NO-GO — no new P0/P1 hole in ownership, lifetime, the sentinel state machine, panic rollback, or Release/Acquire publication; the production protocol itself is sound and needs no algorithm change, but two CONTRACT blockers must close before first publish**) is a STATIC read-only audit (no sub-agents; tests, builds, `cargo check`, Clippy, Miri, rustdoc, benchmarks and package/publish commands not run — its own explicitly stated mode).     - **Status:** CLOSED (2026-08-25, same session) — both contract blockers plus three further findings EXECUTED: F1 by task #1369 (`de1e9f7`), F2 by task #1370 (`7b472f8`), F3 by task #1371 (`1034e7e`), F7's doc half by task #1372 (`94743cc`), F5's unsafe-hygiene half by task #1373 (`f70dc7f`). Four P3 findings (F4, F6, F8, F9) not filed as tasks — each duplicates a P3 item 120 already declined for the same reason (owner instruction: file tasks only for concrete target-repo edits; the review itself rates none of them as blocking). Publication remains explicitly UNAUTHORIZED — standing owner instruction ("не заводи задачи на публикацию, я скажу когда публиковать").
    - **The findings, personally re-verified against source before filing — both blockers CONFIRMED, not transcribed:**
      - **F1 (P1 contract/safety, CONFIRMED — and a genuine defect in item 120/F1's own fix) — the `safe inside #[global_allocator]` promise still omitted the mandatory no-unwind/no-reentry caller contract:** `src/lib.rs:33-45` and `:368-391`, `README.md:10-18`, `Cargo.toml:7`, `CHANGELOG.md:14-29`. Task #1365 (`870001e`, item 120's F1) had narrowed the claim to "success paths" — an improvement, but still wrong for the crate's primary advertised use case: the winning `init` closure IS part of a successful call and is arbitrary caller code that may allocate or transitively re-enter the same allocator, so "success paths allocate nothing" is true of the CELL's internals only, never of a whole `get_or_try_init` call. Three further sub-claims personally re-verified, all correct: (a) the rustdoc at `:387-391` promised an unwinding `init` propagates out of `get_or_try_init` — true in general, but [unwinding out of a `GlobalAlloc` method is undefined behaviour](https://doc.rust-lang.org/core/alloc/trait.GlobalAlloc.html#safety), a condition stated nowhere in the crate; (b) task #1365's own remediation advice, «`panic = "abort"` plus a non-allocating `#[panic_handler]`», conflates two link environments — a `std` binary cannot supply its own `#[panic_handler]` at all (the panic runtime belongs to `std`), while a `no_std` binary must; one recipe does not cover both; (c) "two panic paths" is undercounted — the third is the `align_of::<T>() >= 2` runtime panic in `new`/`Default`, which `tests/cell_unit.rs:169-184` already tests. **EXECUTED by task #1369 (2026-08-25, `de1e9f7`):** the cell's guarantee narrowed to what it actually owns; caller obligations moved into a dedicated crate-doc section, "Using this inside a `#[global_allocator]`", reachable from the README example, the crate docs and `get_or_try_init`'s rustdoc, stating all four rules (no allocation, no blocking, no unwind with the `GlobalAlloc` UB rule cited and linked, sentinel-address result = violated precondition); the `std`/`no_std` panic-strategy split stated as two distinct mechanisms; the panic-site count corrected to three. Verified: `rustdoc -D warnings` clean with the new section's anchor confirmed present in the generated `index.html` and resolving from `struct.RacyPtrCell.html`, `fmt` clean, 7/7.
      - **F2 (P2 contract/docs, CONFIRMED — the claim is factually false) — the `OnceLock` comparison rested on wrong semantics in six places:** `Cargo.toml:7`, `CHANGELOG.md:28`, `README.md:17`, `src/lib.rs:25`, `:44`, `:192`. The crate repeatedly asserted `OnceLock` poisons its cell on a failed initialiser and that its `get_or_try_init` "can never recover". Personally verified against the [documented contract](https://doc.rust-lang.org/std/sync/struct.OnceLock.html#method.get_or_try_init) and std's own implementation: on `Err` the cell stays uninitialised and the next call retries; a panicking initialiser behaves the same way — std drives initialisation through `Once::call_once_force`, which deliberately ignores poisoning, so the internal `p.poison()` has no observable effect on retryability through either `get_or_init` or `get_or_try_init`. `core::cell::OnceCell` likewise does not poison. This was a competitive framing a downstream consumer could reasonably have designed against, and `Cargo.toml:7` freezes at first publish. Note the review cited four surfaces; a `grep` for `poison` across the crate found **six** individual lines, the extra two being `src/lib.rs:44` and `RollbackGuard`'s own doc at `:192` (the latter inside the review's cited `:184-193` range). **EXECUTED by task #1370 (2026-08-25, `7b472f8`):** all six corrected and replaced with the real distinctions (`no_std`, no parking of losing threads, no internal allocation, raw-pointer/lifetime posture, per-caller `Option` rather than a shared `Result`), plus the previously-unmentioned fact that `OnceLock::get_or_try_init` is still unstable (`once_cell_try`). The corrected text states the true `OnceLock` behaviour explicitly so the error cannot be silently re-introduced. Verified: post-fix `grep` for `poison` leaves only the two mentions inside the new corrective paragraph, both correct statements.
      - **F3 (P2 liveness contract, CONFIRMED — second independent flag) — the reentrancy warning forbade only direct same-cell re-entry, not a transitive cycle:** `src/lib.rs:368-376`; README did not state the restriction at all next to its usage example. Two cells deadlock with no self-recursion anywhere: thread 1 wins `A` and its `init` initialises `B` while thread 2 wins `B` and its `init` initialises `A`; both spin forever at 100% CPU. An allocator bootstrap is exactly this shape — `src/registry/bootstrap.rs` already runs 64 per-chunk cells plus an `alloc-xthread` overflow-sidecar instance of the same protocol. **Filed despite item 120 rating the identical point P3 (its F9)** precisely because two independent reviewers now flag it. **EXECUTED by task #1371 (2026-08-25, `1034e7e`):** documented in three places — `get_or_try_init`'s rustdoc (restriction is transitive; several cells form a lock-order graph; acquire in a fixed global order as with locks), the crate-level `#[global_allocator]` section, and the README immediately after its usage example, framed as applying to every user rather than only allocator authors.
      - **F7 (P3 perf/maintainability — DOC HALF ONLY filed, CONFIRMED) — the claim-CAS's success-ordering comment gave an incoherent explanation:** `src/lib.rs:410-413` (pre-fix). The comment claimed the success `Acquire` "pairs with a later winner's `Release` publish observed by future `Acquire` readers". Personally re-verified the text verbatim and re-derived the ordering: an acquire cannot synchronise with a release that has not yet happened, and the claim CAS's ordering constrains no future reader's loads. What the `Acquire` actually does — confirmed by enumerating every `Ordering` site in the file — is synchronise-with the `Release` rollback store of a PREVIOUS abandoning winner (the explicit OOM path at `:544` or the unwind guard's `Drop` at `:299`, the only prior `Release` stores that can leave the cell `null`). **EXECUTED by task #1372 (2026-08-25, `94743cc`):** comment corrected to state the real pairing, to state that the load-bearing Release/Acquire pair for the pointee is the winner's own publish against every reader's load, and to record "whether `Relaxed` would suffice" as an OPEN perf question rather than answering it. **The perf half — actually changing `Acquire` → `Relaxed` on the success CAS — was deliberately NOT filed:** it needs a loom run plus a weak-architecture measurement, and is already tracked as item 119's F5 and item 120's F6.
      - **F5 (P3 integration/unsafe hygiene — HYGIENE HALF filed, CONFIRMED — second independent flag) — three `NonNull::new_unchecked` sites in the root `loom_shim` carry no `// SAFETY:` proof, in a file whose own header asserts every `unsafe` block has one:** `src/registry/bootstrap.rs:320`, `:334`, `:378`, against the header claim at `:187` immediately preceding the tier-1 lift `#![allow(unsafe_code)]` at `:188`. The review ALSO independently re-verified the shim's behavioural fidelity and found **no new drift** since item 119/F2's fix (`29c2b2f`) — matching item 120/F8's own line-by-line finding, a second confirmation. Item 120's F8 raised the identical hygiene point at P3; filed here because two independent reviewers now flag it and CLAUDE.md's "Active rules" makes the grep-based tier-1 seam audit the FORMAL procedure, which would find three unjustified blocks in a file promising none. **EXECUTED by task #1373 (2026-08-25, `f70dc7f`):** all three annotated, mirroring the real crate's own wording at `crates/racy-ptr-cell/src/lib.rs:468-471` and its loser-path site; verified by a `--cfg loom` compile plus `cargo fmt --check`. **The second half of F5 — the shim spins on `core` atomics with a real busy-spin, so a future root loom harness reaching a chunk cell would HANG rather than fail, with the prohibition existing only in a comment (`:240-241`) — was NOT filed:** it needs a mechanical fail-loud boundary (or the deeper fix the review prefers, extracting a shared protocol core so the const-adapter stops duplicating the algorithm), which is a design task, not a doc edit.
      - **P3s NOT filed (F4, F6, F8, F9) — each duplicates a P3 item 120 already declined, for the same stated reason:** F4 — the loom test still round-trips a pointer through `usize` (item 120's F10), and no test observes `get()` concurrently with an active initialiser (item 120's F5). F6 — `FnMut` → `FnOnce`, `dbg_rollback_reenterable`'s unreachable `Some(false)`, `dbg_is_ready` duplicating `get().is_some()`, and `Default`'s undocumented panic (item 119's F4, item 120's F11.5; the unreachable-`Some(false)` observation is genuinely new but is an API-shape call, not a defect). F8 — the cold benchmark measures the system allocator and its own monotonic leak (item 120's F7). F9 — `#![cfg_attr(not(test), no_std)]` making the headline property conditional, and README citing root-repo-only files a crates.io reader cannot follow (item 120's F12 and F11.3/F11.4).
    - **What the review confirmed clean (quoted from its own "Что в реализации сделано хорошо"):** the sentinel is built via `without_provenance_mut` and never dereferenced; the release-active collision assert closes the safe-code-reachable address `1`; `RollbackGuard` correctly clears the sentinel on unwind and the explicit `None` rollback does not rely on implicit `Drop`; the loser re-race after `null` is correct and does not wait for a `READY` that may never come; `Release` publish + `Acquire` observation give the needed happens-before for pointee writes; unconditional `Send + Sync` are justified by the `AtomicPtr` model since the crate never creates a `&T`; `dbg_rollback_reenterable` restores `null` only after re-winning its own CAS and does not clobber a concurrent owner; the real type is modelled by loom atomics with counterfactuals checking non-vacuousness of both main protocol claims; the normal dependency surface is minimal, and no-std cross-build, debug/release tests, all-targets Clippy and a separate loom job are all statically present in CI.
    - **Next trigger:** none open on the five filed findings (all EXECUTED). Still open and deliberately deferred: the perf half of F7 (`Relaxed` claim CAS — needs loom + weak-arch measurement), the loom-boundary half of F5 (design task), and the four unfiled P3s above. Publication itself is gated on an explicit owner go-ahead (not given) plus item 28's crate-name/description decision. Per the review's own closing statement, after F1 and F2 it sees no production reason to block publication, and a repeat STATIC contract review would suffice rather than a full re-audit.
    - **Evidence:** the report itself (`docs/reviews/2026-08-25-164200-racy-ptr-cell-publication-audit-run-2-Sol-codex.md`, commit `1481efc`, committed by the reviewing process); F1 verified against `src/lib.rs:33-45`/`:368-391`, `README.md:10-18`, `Cargo.toml:7`, `CHANGELOG.md:14-29` and the `GlobalAlloc` safety contract (pre-fix); F2 verified by a `grep` for `poison` across the whole crate returning all six lines, cross-checked against `OnceLock::get_or_try_init`'s documented contract and std's `call_once_force`-based implementation; F3 verified against `src/lib.rs:368-376` and the README's example region (pre-fix); F7's doc half verified by reading `src/lib.rs:410-413` verbatim and enumerating every `Ordering::` site in the file (`:299` and `:544` are the only prior `Release` stores that leave the cell `null`); F5's hygiene half verified by reading `src/registry/bootstrap.rs:317-384` in full plus the header claim at `:187`. All five fixes independently re-verified post-edit: `RUSTDOCFLAGS="-D warnings" cargo doc -p racy-ptr-cell --no-deps`, `cargo fmt --check`, `cargo clippy -p racy-ptr-cell --all-targets -- -D warnings`, `cargo test -p racy-ptr-cell --no-fail-fast` 7/7, and for #1373 a `RUSTFLAGS="--cfg loom" cargo check --features alloc-global,internals --tests` compile.
    - **Independence and mode.** Static read-only, one agent, no sub-agents, nothing executable run — the review's own stated mode, which means every claim is a code-reading conclusion and none of its "confirmed clean" statements is an execution receipt. Two failure scenarios, both textbook and neither requiring a misbehaving `init`: (a) `fork()` in a multithreaded process — the child inherits a cell stuck at `INITIALIZING` with no thread able to publish or roll it back (every subsequent caller in the child spins forever; `dbg_rollback_reenterable`'s entry CAS requires the cell to already be `null`, so it is a structural no-op here, by design); (b) an allocating signal handler — the handler re-enters the same allocator, the claim CAS fails, and the handler spins on a sentinel owned by the very thread it interrupted, an unrecoverable single-thread self-deadlock. The in-repo consumer is exactly this shape: `src/registry/bootstrap.rs` puts 64 `RacyPtrCell` instances on the root crate's own `#[global_allocator]` bootstrap path. **EXECUTED by task #1374 (2026-08-25, `0835d8d`):** the latency claim qualified ("provided a winner is currently running at all") in both `lib.rs` and `README.md`; a new "Fork and signal safety" subsection added to both, stating the hazard and the caller mitigation (publish every cell before the first `fork()`, or `fork()` only from a thread known to hold no cell plus mandatory `exec()` in the child; never allocate in a signal handler). Documentation only — an `unsafe fn reset_after_fork` was explicitly considered by the review and deferred as a separate API-surface decision, not folded into this fix. Verified: `rustdoc -D warnings` clean (new anchor resolves), `fmt` clean, `cargo test -p racy-ptr-cell` 7/7.

Both locally re-verified before committing (7/7 each).

Consequence inside a `#[global_allocator]`: the panic hook's allocation re-enters the same mid-`init` cell, the CAS fails, and the thread spins on a sentinel it owns itself — an unrecoverable self-deadlock, `abort()` never reached, and the `assert!`'s diagnostic (the entire point of task #707 making it release-active) never reaches stderr because the allocation that would have printed it is the one that deadlocked. The repository's own root crate already pays this bill — a ~200-line `loom_shim` in `src/registry/bootstrap.rs` that two prior reviews (item 119/F2, item 121/F5) had to audit for drift — with no equivalent guidance offered to any other downstream consumer. **EXECUTED by task #1377 (2026-08-25, `a7cbc6c`):** README's loom command now scopes with `-p racy-ptr-cell` plus a new warning paragraph; a matching "CONSUMER HAZARD" comment added next to the atomics-alias in `lib.rs`; `new()`'s own rustdoc states the hazard and both mitigations (scope the flag, or supply a const-capable `#[cfg(loom)]` stand-in) instead of only naming the internal mechanism. The review's OPTIONAL second-half suggestion (feature-gate the loom aliasing behind `#[cfg(all(loom, feature = "loom"))]`) was deliberately NOT done — a genuine one-way-door API decision the review itself frames as separate. F4 — the CHANGELOG's "zero non-`std` dependencies" is true of compilation, false of the RESOLVED dependency graph: a scratch consumer measured **31 packages** locked (loom's tree, including `cc`), a real but non-blocking precision gap in a deliberate, defensible design tradeoff (loom must be a library, not dev, dependency for the crate's own headline "loom proofs against the real type" claim to hold). F5 — the fork mitigation text (item 122/F1's own fix, `0835d8d`) checks the WRONG thread's cell-holding status (the forking thread's, when the hazard is what OTHER threads hold) and pairs a general cell-level claim with an allocator-only rule, when non-allocator consumers (the crate's own advertised "runtimes, bare-metal bootstraps" audience) need the stricter "never call `get_or_try_init` from a signal handler" framing; a residual imprecision in already-fixed text, not a fresh defect. F6 — no `Debug` impl (Rust API Guidelines C-COMMON-TRAITS); purely additive, does not close at upload, lowest-value item in the report.

    - **The findings, personally re-verified against source before filing — both P2s CONFIRMED, not transcribed:**
      - **F1 (P2, CONFIRMED, MEASURED AND REPRODUCED AS AN ACTUAL HANG — the fourth revision of this paragraph, second consecutive cycle where the recommended remedy did not do what it claimed) — the `std::panic::set_hook` mitigation item 123's F1 added closes the hazard only for a bare `&'static str` panic message:** `src/lib.rs:138-149` (the `std` bullet added by `64060b4`), `README.md:86-95`. Personally re-verified the quoted paragraph matches `src/lib.rs:146-149` verbatim. Mechanism, from the active toolchain's own `library/std/src/panicking.rs:832-836`: `payload.get()` is evaluated as an ARGUMENT to the hook call, before the hook body, on both the default and custom hook arms; for a formatted message the payload is `FormatStringPayload`, whose `get()` materialises a `String` via allocation (`fill()`, `:629-639`) — only a bare-`&'static str` payload (`StaticStrPayload::get`) avoids it, and there is no hook shape that can intercept an allocation upstream of the hook call. Measured on one binary with the crate's full recommended recipe (non-allocating `set_hook` + `panic = "abort"`): bare `&'static str` → 0 allocations before the hook; `panic!("… {}", <runtime>)` → 2; `Result::unwrap()` on `Err` → 2; `assert_eq!` → 4. REPRODUCED end-to-end as the actual failure mode, not merely argued: a real `#[global_allocator]` bootstrapping a `static CELL: RacyPtrCell<Chunk>`, the crate's full recommended recipe applied, `init` panicking, both arms run under `timeout 15` — static message: exit 50 (hook reached, clean); formatted message: **exit 124 (HUNG)**, the exact self-deadlock the paragraph itself warns about, produced by following the paragraph's own advice in full. In mitigation: the crate's own three panic sites all use static argument-free messages, so the recipe genuinely is complete for those — the gap is confined to the one panic site (a caller's `init`) the crate does not control, which is also the entire subject of the `#[global_allocator]` section. **EXECUTED by task #1378 (2026-08-25, `460191f`):** both `lib.rs` and `README.md` gained an explicit "Residual limit" clause naming the constraint, with the measured allocation counts, and stating the only complete mitigation for a formatted message is an `init` that cannot panic. Also folded in, from the same paragraph's still-open carryover (item 123's cycle-3 F3): a note that `panic = "abort"` compiles the crate's internal rollback guard out entirely (unwind-only), so under that profile the cell-consistency guarantee comes from the process dying, not the guard. Caught and fixed a NEW broken intra-doc link this very edit introduced (`` [`RollbackGuard`] `` — a private type, unlinkable from public docs even with a valid path; replaced with a plain code span) before committing — directly the same defect CLASS as F2 below, caught this time only because the fix was run through the exact command F2 was about to make mandatory. (Filed 2026-08-26, this session.)
    - **Status:** CLOSED -- all nine findings EXECUTED across eight commits: F1/#1387 (`3b58bb1`), F2+F8/#1388 (`4ba0ec4`), F3/#1389 (`e5f018f`), F4/#1390 (`d1379e7`), F7/#1392 (`4c70e26`), F6/#1391 (`9374078`), F8's last bullet/#1394 (`6bce17e`), F5/#1393 (`79da4d5`), plus item 130's carryover N3/#1395 (`86e9a83`) folded into the same wave.     - **F1 (P1, release blocker, CONFIRMED -- and this session's OWN defect, introduced two hours earlier by task #1384):** `src/lib.rs:180-199`, `README.md:73-92`. The fork guidance task #1384 landed (`ea25359`, closing item 129's F1) split the advice into two "disciplines", the first of which said a process-wide barrier serializing `fork()` against every initializer is SUFFICIENT when the child touches the allocator or cell before `exec()`. It is not: POSIX.1-2024 requires the child of a multithreaded process to call only async-signal-safe functions until a successful `exec()`. The barrier proves only this protocol's own local invariant (no `RacyPtrCell` holds the sentinel at fork time) and does nothing about inherited allocator/runtime locks, nor does it make `get_or_try_init`, the `init` closure, or any panic path async-signal-safe -- following the published advice could deadlock or invoke UB entirely outside this cell. **EXECUTED by task #1387 (`3b58bb1`):** rewritten so the POSIX rule IS the rule with the crate adding nothing to it; the barrier demoted to exactly what it does (prevents a child snapshotting a cell wedged at `INITIALIZING`) with an explicit "does NOT authorize allocator/cell/runtime calls in the child"; anything broader named as an environment-specific contract the consumer owns, requiring a proven `atfork` protocol. Also states the barrier protocol unambiguously (initializers hold shared across `init`; the forking thread takes exclusive and holds it ACROSS the `fork()` call, not merely until quiescence) -- which closes item 130's N5 in the same edit. Both POSIX citations verified to render as real anchors in the generated HTML, not literal text.
    - **The eight P3 groups, all closed:**
      - **F2 (three panic-path contradictions) + F8 (eight doc/maintainability bullets) -- task #1388 (`4ba0ec4`):** the `no_std` bullet's "supplies a non-allocating `#[panic_handler]`" vs "no handler, no allocation" self-negation (open since item 124's F3(b)); the "guarantee **below**" pointing 57 lines up (item 128's F1); "The rules above are all about what `init` does" being false about its own antecedent after task #1383's reorder (item 128's F2); the claim-CAS comment's stale `:544` reference AND its false "the only prior `Release` stores" claim, which omitted `dbg_rollback_reenterable`'s two null-stores (= item 130's N1); `OnceLock` "parks" changed to "may block" in `lib.rs`, `README.md` and `Cargo.toml`'s description (blocking is std's documented contract; parking is an implementation detail); `#![cfg_attr(not(test), no_std)]` made an unconditional `#![no_std]`; the unsafe-seam header's "SINGLE reason" vs "two audited kinds" self-contradiction; the type doc's retired "safe inside a `#[global_allocator]`" changed to "usable inside"; the loom suite's own run command gaining `-p racy-ptr-cell` (= item 130's N4); and internal `task #...`/finding numbers/`CLAUDE.md` references/root-repo paths stripped from public rustdoc and README.

Measured: cold 73.62 ns/op (baseline 60.18), get/hot 2.98, warm 3.67, contention 9252.67 (baseline 6160.19, 4 threads); the cold row's calibration moved from 4.4M to 6.9M iters/s, consistent with the allocation having left the timed region.

Loom suite is now 8 tests.

The implementation moved verbatim to `src/imp.rs` under a POSITIVE `#[cfg(target_has_atomic = "ptr")]`, with `lib.rs` keeping docs, attributes, the guard and two re-exports -- a relocation diff, not a whole-file reindent. **Measured on the real target:** `--target thumbv6m-none-eabi` now emits exactly the named `compile_error!` and nothing else, so the stronger INSTEAD-of wording (weakened by task #1385 precisely because it was false) is restored as true. The root repo's `tests/dbg_hook_safety_tripwire.rs` CAUGHT the file move on its own ("NEW unaccounted-for SAFE hooks: .../imp.rs::dbg_is_ready / Listed SAFE hooks no longer found: .../lib.rs::dbg_is_ready") -- the drift signal it exists to produce -- and its allowlist plus the root crate's unsafe-seam inventory were updated in the same commit.

Measured via the PAIRED difference `warm_already_ready` minus `get/hot` (which cancels this host's clock drift, since single-run absolutes span 2.95-3.26 ns/op for `get/hot` alone): **before +0.69/+0.41 ns/op, after -0.04/+0.18/-0.31/-0.16** -- going through `get_or_try_init` on a READY cell no longer costs more than a bare `get()`, which is exactly the mechanism (the two hot paths are now the same instruction sequence). Absolute gain is about 0.4 ns/op, below per-run noise; no larger claim made. **The two ordering candidates were NOT acted on, by design:** weakening the claim CAS's success `Acquire` or the loser spin's per-iteration `Acquire` needs both a loom counterfactual AND a measurement showing benefit, and the second is unobtainable on x86-64 where `Acquire` is a plain load -- a local A/B can only report noise. 132. **[T, SIXTEENTH independent review of `racy-ptr-cell`, fifth Sol-codex-authored run] `docs/reviews/2026-08-26-070854-racy-ptr-cell-publication-audit-run-5-Sol-codex.md`** (author Sol-codex, static read-only -- no `cargo`/Miri/loom/rustdoc/bench/`cargo package`/publish-dry-run executed, no sub-agents; reported 2026-08-26 07:08:54 +02:00; revision `782b6fa`; verdict **NO-GO on one P2 layout-contract defect; six further P3 groups**). Continues the "fix everything" instruction from item 131's cycle. Confirmed item 131's own P1 fix (`3b58bb1`, the fork-contract rewrite) fully correct and closed. (Filed 2026-08-26, this session.)
    - **Status:** CLOSED -- all seven findings EXECUTED across seven commits: F1/#1396 (`db3c79b`), F2/#1397 (`2745cd5`), F3/#1398 (`714393a`), F4/#1399 (`98f29ae`), F5/#1400 (`46674c0`), F6/#1401 (`655154a`), F7/#1402 (`d6511da`). Publication remains explicitly UNAUTHORIZED.
    - **F1 (P2, release blocker, CONFIRMED) -- the crate repeatedly promises "one `AtomicPtr`"/"the cell is one word" but `RacyPtrCell<T>` was plain `repr(Rust)`:** `src/imp.rs:74-90` (pre-fix), `README.md:3-9,163-166`, `CHANGELOG.md:15-18`, `Cargo.toml:7`. For `repr(Rust)`, field order/padding/single-field size equivalence are not part of the contract; the current compiler almost certainly lays the type out as one word, but a published guarantee should not rest on an unstated layout detail a consumer building allocator metadata or an array of cells is entitled to rely on. **EXECUTED by task #1396 (`db3c79b`):** added `#[repr(transparent)]` (a pure annotation -- `PhantomData<*mut T>` is always zero-sized with alignment 1, exactly what the attribute requires beyond the one real field); added `layout_matches_a_single_atomic_ptr` (`tests/cell_unit.rs`) asserting `size_of`/`align_of` equality against `AtomicPtr<T>` -- guards against a LATER edit widening the struct while still compiling, not against the attribute itself (a wrong `repr(transparent)` is a compile error, not a runtime bug); mirrored the attribute onto the root crate's `#[cfg(loom)]` shim; added a new "Layout" doc section to `lib.rs`/`README.md`/`CHANGELOG.md`. Verified: full crate gate green, root crate clippy green, `RUSTFLAGS="--cfg loom" cargo check -p sefer-alloc` green, `cargo package --allow-dirty` with verify build (13 files, clean).
    - **The six P3 groups, all closed:**
      - **F2 (`RollbackProbe::NotApplicable` overclaimed unconditional state preservation) -- task #1397 (`2745cd5`):** `src/imp.rs:65-70`. "In both cases the probe leaves the cell exactly as it found it" was true for the untouched-on-entry case but literally false for the raced-and-lost case -- the cell may be `INITIALIZING` or `READY` by the time `NotApplicable` returns, not the `UNINIT` the probe originally observed. Method-level docs already got this right; the enum variant doc now matches.
      - **F3 (`baseline/barriers_only` still ran the cell protocol in worker threads) -- task #1398 (`714393a`), this session's own bug from task #1391:** `benches/racy_ptr_cell_bench.rs`. Contenders ran an unconditional `loop { c.round(mine) }` regardless of which row the benchmark thread was timing, so the "baseline" secretly still paid for mutex lock + `Arc` clone + warm `get_or_try_init` on three threads -- the contention-minus-baseline difference was not the cell protocol's own contended cost, as claimed. **EXECUTED:** added an explicit per-round mode (`Contend`/`BarriersOnly`/`Shutdown`) set by the benchmark thread immediately before its own barrier crossing; `BarriersOnly` contenders now touch nothing but the two barrier waits; `Shutdown` lets the process join its workers for real (one more `start` crossing releases them, then they are joined) instead of the previous "reaped at process exit" design. Recalibrated: `baseline/barriers_only`'s iteration count rose from 156855 to 199167, consistent with genuinely less work per round.
      - **F4 (the loom test's own name overclaimed a temporal property it never asserted) -- task #1399 (`98f29ae`), this session's own test from task #1392:** `tests/loom_racy_ptr_cell.rs:395-465`. `real_get_returns_none_while_a_winner_holds_the_sentinel` loaded `in_init` into `_init_started` and never used the value, accepting either `None` or a valid `Some` unconditionally -- it proved the weaker "get() never reports null/sentinel as Some" property, not the temporal "None WHILE a winner holds the sentinel" claim in its own name. **EXECUTED:** rewritten as a real two-sided handshake (winner announces `in_init` then blocks for the reader's go-ahead before publishing; reader waits for `in_init`, then asserts `get()` is STRICTLY `None`, then releases the winner) -- **re-proven non-vacuous by the same fault-injection method used for the original test**: weakening `is_ready` to `a != 0` now makes this test fail directly on its own strict assertion (`left: Some(0x1)`), where the old version would likely have passed vacuously on that interleaving.
      - **F5 (the already-self-documented liveness gap: unwind while a loser is already spinning) -- task #1400 (`46674c0`):** `src/imp.rs:129-144` (comment), `tests/cell_unit.rs`. The `RollbackGuard` doc already named this exact gap -- the existing panic-rollback test proves only that a SUBSEQUENT caller can init after an unwind, never a loser thread already spinning at the moment of it. Current unconditional rollback is correct today (not a known bug), but a future conditional rollback ("skip if no loser observed waiting") could pass the existing test while reintroducing the livelock on exactly the path that matters most. **EXECUTED:** new native test `unwind_while_a_loser_is_already_spinning_wakes_it_and_it_wins` -- a deterministic handshake (the loser only calls `get_or_try_init` after observing the winner already holds the sentinel; the winner only unwinds after observing the loser is about to call) makes the loser's first CAS attempt provably see the live sentinel. Verified with 5 repeated release runs, no flake; follows the file's own established bounded-timeout-not-unconditional-join pattern for the failure path.
      - **F6 (`CHANGELOG.md` stale against the API/doc changes) -- task #1401 (`655154a`):** three gaps: `OnceLock` still described as "parks" (README/lib.rs/Cargo.toml already corrected to "may block" by item 131's F8); no mention anywhere of `RollbackProbe`, `FnOnce`, or `#[must_use]`, all landed this session; internal `task #706`/`#707`/`#710` IDs still present. **EXECUTED:** all three fixed; the `get_or_try_init` bullet now states the `FnOnce`/`#[must_use]` signature, the test-probe bullet names `RollbackProbe` and its two variants, internal IDs removed.
      - **F7 (no package/publish-dry-run gate in ordinary CI) -- task #1402 (`d6511da`):** `sefer-region`/`aligned-vmem` each have a dedicated "package gates" job running `cargo publish --dry-run` on every PR; `racy-ptr-cell` had none anywhere in `ci.yml` -- a tarball-content or manifest-normalization regression would surface only at tag/release time. **EXECUTED:** added `racy-ptr-cell-gates`, mirroring `sefer-region-gates`'s shape, with `cargo publish --dry-run -p racy-ptr-cell` only (no `cargo semver-checks` step -- unlike `sefer-region`/`aligned-vmem`, this crate has never been published, so no crates.io baseline exists yet to diff against; add that step in a future task once the first publish lands). Verified: the exact command runs clean locally (13 files packaged, verify build succeeds), the job's YAML parses correctly.
    - **Next trigger:** none open on any of this review's seven findings. Same three owner-gated pre-publish items as items 128/130/131 (explicit publish go-ahead -- still not given; item 28's metadata decision; the CHANGELOG date-stamp, a hard `release.yml` gate).
